8.5 AWS Compute Decision Flow — What to Use When

The most common question: "Which compute service should I use?" This decision tree answers it:

❓ START: Do you want to manage servers?
▼ YES
▼ NO
Need full OS control or specific hardware?
▼ YES
▼ NO
✅ EC2 — Full control, any OS, any software
✅ EKS — Kubernetes (or ECS for simpler)
Running containers or just code?
▼ Containers
▼ Just Code
✅ Fargate — Serverless containers (ECS/EKS)
✅ Lambda — Event-driven, pay-per-use
ServiceControlScalingPricingBest For
EC2Full OSASG (manual rules)Per second (instance)Legacy apps, custom OS config
ECSContainer-levelService Auto ScalingEC2 or Fargate pricingAWS-only, simpler than K8s
EKSKubernetes APIHPA, VPA, Cluster Autoscaler$0.10/hr + worker nodesMicroservices, multi-cloud, K8s ecosystem
LambdaNone (code only)Automatic (per-invocation)Per ms + requestsEvent-driven, sporadic workloads
FargateContainer spec onlyService Auto ScalingPer task per secondContainers without managing servers
💡 THE DEVOPS RULE FOR COMPUTE

Start serverless, go to containers when needed, fall back to EC2 only when you must. Lambda/Fargate → less ops. ECS/EKS → orchestration. EC2 → maximum control, maximum responsibility. The exam answer is almost always the one requiring LESS operational overhead.

🔑 Compute is NOT about "which is better"

Every AWS compute service solves a DIFFERENT problem. The exam tests matching workload to service. Lambda at 14 minutes = WRONG (15-min limit). EC2 running 100ms/day = WRONG (paying for idle). Right-size your compute.

🏢 REAL-WORLD COMPUTE — Who Runs What on AWS
🛒 Amazon.com — EC2 + ASG

Millions of EC2 instances

Stack: EC2 (custom Nitro instances) → Auto Scaling Groups → Elastic Load Balancing

Why: Full control over hardware for bare-metal performance. ASG scales automatically during Prime Day. Custom kernel optimizations impossible on serverless.

🏦 Coinbase — EKS

100% on Kubernetes

Stack: EKS → Helm charts → HPA + Cluster Autoscaler → Istio service mesh

Why: Crypto markets are 24/7 global. Zero-downtime rolling updates. HPA auto-scales during volatility. K8s ecosystem (Helm, Istio, Argo) essential for compliance.

📺 Disney+ — Lambda

100M+ subscribers

Stack: Lambda (video encoding) → S3 (media storage) → CloudFront (global delivery)

Why: Launch-day spikes (Hamilton premiere) handled without provisioning. Pay only for actual encoding time. Serverless = zero capacity planning for unpredictable streaming demand.

🚕 Lyft — ECS + Fargate

1000s of microservices

Stack: ECS Fargate → Service Auto Scaling → ALB → DynamoDB (ride tracking)

Why: Serverless containers = no node management. ECS is AWS-native (no K8s learning curve). Sub-second dispatch requires consistent latency — Fargate delivers.

8.6 Deploying anihpj on EC2 — The Traditional Way

Before Kubernetes, DevOps engineers deployed apps directly on EC2. Here's how you'd deploy the anihpj Django app on EC2 using a bootstrap script:

EC2 User Data — Bootstrap Script for anihpj
#!/bin/bash
# User Data script — runs ONCE when EC2 first launches
# This automates the entire anihpj setup

# 1. Update system & install Docker
yum update -y
amazon-linux-extras install docker -y
service docker start
usermod -a -G docker ec2-user
systemctl enable docker

# 2. Install Docker Compose
curl -L "https://github.com/docker/compose/releases/latest/download/docker-compose-$(uname -s)-$(uname -m)" -o /usr/local/bin/docker-compose
chmod +x /usr/local/bin/docker-compose

# 3. Install nginx as reverse proxy
amazon-linux-extras install nginx1 -y

# 4. Pull anihpj from ECR (authenticated via IAM Role)
aws ecr get-login-password --region us-east-1 | docker login --username AWS --password-stdin 123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com
docker pull 123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:latest

# 5. Create docker-compose.yml on the fly
cat > /home/ec2-user/docker-compose.yml << 'EOF'
version: '3.8'
services:
  web:
    image: 123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:latest
    ports:
      - "8000:8000"
    environment:
      DJANGO_SETTINGS_MODULE: anihpj.settings
      DATABASE_URL: postgres://user:pass@anihpj-db.xxx.rds.amazonaws.com:5432/anihpj
    restart: always
EOF

# 6. Start anihpj
cd /home/ec2-user
docker-compose up -d

# 7. Configure nginx as reverse proxy
cat > /etc/nginx/conf.d/anihpj.conf << 'EOF'
server {
    listen 80;
    server_name _;
    location / {
        proxy_pass http://127.0.0.1:8000;
        proxy_set_header Host $host;
        proxy_set_header X-Real-IP $remote_addr;
    }
    location /static/ {
        alias /home/ec2-user/static/;
    }
}
EOF
systemctl enable nginx
systemctl start nginx

echo "✅ anihpj is running on port 80!"
📝 Key DevOps Principles in This Script

1. Automation: Zero manual steps — launch EC2, and anihpj is running. 2. No hardcoded secrets: ECR auth uses the EC2 IAM Role (no passwords!). 3. Immutable: If the instance has issues, terminate and launch a new one — the script rebuilds everything. 4. This is how we did it before Kubernetes. With EKS, these steps move into Dockerfile + Deployment YAML.

1
Launch EC2

AMI + User Data

2
Install Docker

User Data script

3
Pull Image

From ECR via IAM Role

4
Start Container

docker-compose up -d

5
Configure nginx

Reverse proxy :80→:8000

📊 Chapter 8 — Recap: AWS Compute Services

🖥️EC2Virtual servers, full OS control, ASG for resilience
📦ECSAWS-native containers, simpler than Kubernetes
☸️EKSManaged Kubernetes, K8s ecosystem, multi-cloud
⚡LambdaServerless functions, event-driven, pay-per-use
☁️FargateServerless containers, no instance management
🌳Decision TreeServer mgmt? → Control? → Containers? → Code?

🧠 Chapter 8 — Core Takeaways

  • ✅ EC2 = maximum control, maximum responsibility. Use for legacy apps, custom OS.
  • ✅ Lambda = zero ops, event-driven. Cost-effective for sporadic. NOT for long-running.
  • ✅ ECS = simpler than K8s, AWS-only. Free control plane. No K8s learning curve.
  • ✅ EKS = the Kubernetes standard. $0.10/hr. Helm, Istio, multi-cloud portability.
  • ✅ Fargate = containers without servers. No nodes to manage, pay per task.
⏱️ 20 minutes
Scenario: Choose the Right Compute for anihpj Components

anihpj has these components: (1) Django web server (long-running, needs Docker), (2) Resume image processor (triggered on S3 upload), (3) Nightly job cleanup (scheduled at 2 AM), (4) PostgreSQL database. For each component, choose the BEST AWS compute service and justify your choice.

💡 Hint: Django = needs containers, always running. Image processor = event-driven, sporadic. Nightly task = scheduled, short-lived. Database = managed service, not compute at all.
▼ Solution (Click to Reveal) 
# 1. Django Web Server → EKS (or ECS/EC2)
#    Long-running, needs Docker, needs orchestration (scaling, updates)
#    EKS recommended: K8s ecosystem, multi-cloud portable, large community

# 2. Resume Image Processor → Lambda
#    Event-driven (S3 upload triggers it), sporadic workload
#    Pay only when processing, no idle costs, auto-scales to zero

# 3. Nightly Job Cleanup → Lambda + CloudWatch Events
#    Scheduled (cron), short-lived (< 15 min), no server needed
#    Or: Kubernetes CronJob if already using EKS

# 4. PostgreSQL Database → RDS (not a compute decision!)
#    Managed database service — AWS handles scaling, backups, patching
#    NOT a compute choice — it's a data tier decision

📝 Chapter 8 — AWS DevOps Practice Questions (10 Questions)

Q1

You need to run the anihpj Django application with full control over the OS, including custom kernel modules. Which AWS compute service should you choose?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

EC2 (Elastic Compute Cloud). EC2 gives you full root/administrator access, allowing custom kernel modules, specific OS configurations, and any software installation. No other AWS compute service provides this level of control.

📖 EXPLANATION

Lambda is serverless — no OS access. Fargate manages the underlying OS. ECS/EKS give container-level control only. EC2 is the only option when you need kernel-level customization. Use EC2 with Auto Scaling Groups for production resilience.

Q2

Your team is migrating from an on-premises Kubernetes cluster to AWS. You want to keep using Kubernetes tools (Helm, kubectl, Istio) and maintain multi-cloud portability. Which service should you use?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Amazon EKS (Elastic Kubernetes Service). EKS runs standard, upstream Kubernetes — your existing Helm charts, kubectl commands, and Istio configuration work without modification.

📖 EXPLANATION

ECS is AWS-native — completely different API, no kubectl, no Helm. With EKS, you maintain K8s portability: move between AWS, GCP, Azure, or on-premises using the same manifests and tools. EKS is the right choice when Kubernetes expertise already exists in the team.

Q3

A startup wants to run containerized microservices on AWS without learning Kubernetes. They need service discovery, load balancing, and auto-scaling — but want to stay within the AWS ecosystem. Which service fits best?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Amazon ECS (Elastic Container Service). ECS provides native container orchestration with service discovery (Cloud Map), load balancing (ALB integration), and auto-scaling — all using AWS concepts, not Kubernetes.

📖 EXPLANATION

ECS has a gentler learning curve than EKS. Task Definitions replace Pod specs, Services replace K8s Services. The control plane is FREE (unlike EKS at $0.10/hr). For AWS-only teams who don't need multi-cloud, ECS is often the simpler, cheaper choice.

Q4

You need to process user-uploaded resume files for anihpj. Processing takes 3 seconds per file, happens unpredictably throughout the day (0-100 uploads/hour), and involves calling AWS Rekognition. Which compute service minimizes cost?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

AWS Lambda. Lambda charges per millisecond of execution — you pay nothing when there are no uploads. With EC2/Fargate, you'd pay for idle capacity 24/7. Lambda also integrates natively with S3 triggers.

📖 EXPLANATION
# Lambda function — triggered by S3 upload
import boto3
def handler(event, context):
    bucket = event['Records'][0]['s3']['bucket']['name']
    key = event['Records'][0]['s3']['object']['key']
    # Process resume with Rekognition
    rekognition = boto3.client('rekognition')
    # ... process and save results ...
    # Cost: ~$0.0000002 per invocation + Rekognition API costs
    # If 0 uploads: $0.00!
Q5

You want to run anihpj containers on ECS but don't want to manage any EC2 instances. Which ECS launch type should you use?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Fargate launch type. With Fargate, you define CPU/memory per task, and AWS provisions the infrastructure automatically. No EC2 instances to patch, scale, or secure.

📖 EXPLANATION

ECS has two launch types: EC2 (you manage instances) and Fargate (serverless). Fargate is the DevOps-friendly choice — less operational overhead, per-task isolation, and pay-per-use pricing. Each ECS task gets its own ENI (Elastic Network Interface) for networking isolation.

Q6

Your EC2 Auto Scaling Group has min=2, desired=3, max=6. Three instances are running. One instance fails its health check. What happens?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The ASG terminates the unhealthy instance and launches a new one. After replacement, 3 instances are running again (maintaining the desired count).

📖 EXPLANATION

Auto Scaling Groups continuously perform health checks. If an instance fails (status check or ELB health check), the ASG: (1) marks it unhealthy, (2) terminates it, (3) launches a replacement. This happens automatically — the desired count of 3 is maintained without human intervention. If CPU spikes above your scaling policy threshold, the ASG scales OUT (up to max=6).

Q7

What is a Golden AMI, and why should DevOps engineers use EC2 Image Builder to create one?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

A Golden AMI is a pre-configured machine image with all software, dependencies, and security patches baked in. EC2 Image Builder automates the creation pipeline: base OS → install packages → run tests → produce AMI.

📖 EXPLANATION

Without Golden AMIs, each EC2 launch runs User Data scripts (5-10 minutes to configure). With Golden AMIs, instances launch in seconds — fully configured. Image Builder automates the pipeline: source OS → build components (install Docker, Python, nginx) → test → distribute AMI to regions. This is the DevOps pattern of immutable infrastructure.

Q8

You have a Lambda function that hasn't been invoked for 30 minutes. A new request arrives. What is a "cold start" and how does it affect this invocation?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

A cold start is the initialization latency (~1-3 seconds) when Lambda creates a new execution environment. After 30 minutes of inactivity, the previous environment was recycled, so this invocation experiences a cold start.

📖 EXPLANATION

Lambda execution environments persist for some time after invocation (typically 5-15 minutes). When a new request arrives and no warm environment exists, AWS: (1) downloads your code, (2) initializes the runtime, (3) runs your init code. This adds ~1-3 seconds. After that, subsequent requests reuse the warm environment (no cold start). For latency-sensitive apps, use Provisioned Concurrency to keep environments warm.

Q9

Your anihpj deployment on EC2 needs to pull Docker images from ECR. Where should you store the AWS credentials for aws ecr get-login-password?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Nowhere on the instance! Attach an IAM Role to the EC2 instance with ecr:GetAuthorizationToken and ecr:BatchGetImage permissions. The AWS SDK/CLI automatically retrieves temporary credentials from the instance metadata service.

📖 EXPLANATION
// IAM Role attached to EC2 instance (NO credentials on disk!)
{
  "Version": "2012-10-17",
  "Statement": [{
    "Effect": "Allow",
    "Action": [
      "ecr:GetAuthorizationToken",
      "ecr:BatchCheckLayerAvailability",
      "ecr:BatchGetImage"
    ],
    "Resource": "*"
  }]
}
// EC2 automatically gets temp creds via http://169.254.169.254/latest/meta-data/iam/security-credentials/
// aws ecr get-login-password uses these automatically — no config needed!
Q10

Compare EC2 + Auto Scaling Group vs EKS with Cluster Autoscaler for running anihpj. Which provides better container orchestration and why?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

EKS provides better container orchestration. While both can scale, EKS adds: rolling updates (zero-downtime), self-healing (auto-restart crashed containers), service discovery (DNS), load balancing (Services), configuration management (ConfigMaps/Secrets), and the entire K8s ecosystem (Helm, Istio, ArgoCD).

📖 EXPLANATION
CapabilityEC2 + ASGEKS
ScalingASG (instance-level)HPA (Pod) + Cluster Autoscaler (Node)
UpdatesManual or CFN rollingkubectl rollout (zero downtime)
Self-healingASG replaces instanceRestart container (seconds)
Service DiscoveryManual (ALB targets)Built-in DNS + Services

Chapter 9: AWS Networking — VPC, Subnets & Connectivity

Networking is the backbone of every AWS deployment. If you don't understand VPCs, subnets, and routing, you can't secure your applications. In this chapter, you'll design the network architecture that runs anihpj on EKS — from VPC to Load Balancer to DNS.

🟢 Beginner ⏱️ ~3 hours 🎯 DOP-C02: Resiliency

🎯 What You'll Learn

  • ✅ VPC fundamentals — CIDR, subnets, route tables, gateways
  • ✅ Security Groups vs NACLs — and when to use each
  • ✅ VPC design patterns for EKS (public + private subnets across AZs)
  • ✅ Load Balancers — ALB vs NLB vs GWLB
  • ✅ Route 53 DNS, CloudFront CDN, and API Gateway

9.1 VPC Fundamentals — Your Private Cloud Network

A VPC (Virtual Private Cloud) is your own isolated network in AWS. Think of it as your personal data center — you control IP ranges, subnets, routing, and firewall rules. Every AWS resource (EC2, RDS, EKS) lives inside a VPC.

Complete VPC Architecture — Public & Private Subnets
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  VPC: 10.0.0.0/16 (65,536 IPs) — us-east-1                          │
│                                                                      │
│  ┌───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐  │
│  │  us-east-1a                                                    │  │
│  │  ┌─────────────────────┐  ┌─────────────────────┐             │  │
│  │  │ PUBLIC SUBNET        │  │ PRIVATE SUBNET       │             │  │
│  │  │ 10.0.1.0/24          │  │ 10.0.10.0/24         │             │  │
│  │  │                     │  │                      │             │  │
│  │  │ ┌─────────────┐    │  │ ┌─────────────┐     │             │  │
│  │  │ │ NAT Gateway  │    │  │ │ EKS Worker   │     │             │  │
│  │  │ │ (elastic IP) │    │  │ │ Node         │     │             │  │
│  │  │ └──────┬───────┘    │  │ │ 10.0.10.45   │     │             │  │
│  │  │        │            │  │ └─────────────┘     │             │  │
│  │  │  ┌─────▼────────┐  │  │                      │             │  │
│  │  │  │ ALB (public)  │  │  │ Pods get IPs from    │             │  │
│  │  │  │ internet-     │  │  │ VPC CNI:             │             │  │
│  │  │  │ facing       │  │  │ 10.0.10.100-200      │             │  │
│  │  │  └──────────────┘  │  └─────────────────────┘             │  │
│  │  └─────────────────────┘                                       │  │
│  │  Route Table:            Route Table:                          │  │
│  │  10.0.0.0/16 → local    10.0.0.0/16 → local                   │  │
│  │  0.0.0.0/0 → IGW        0.0.0.0/0 → NAT Gateway              │  │
│  └───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘  │
│                                                                      │
│  Repeat in us-east-1b and us-east-1c for high availability!         │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
🧬 DEEP DIVE: How VPC Isolation Works — Hardware-Level Enforcement

How does AWS ensure your VPC traffic CANNOT be seen by other customers on the same physical hardware? Through three layers of enforced isolation — all the way down to the silicon:

🖥️
LAYER 1: Nitro Card (Hardware)

Every packet is encapsulated with VPC metadata by a dedicated Nitro PCIe card. Functions like a hardware VPN tunnel per VPC. Other customers on the same physical wire cannot sniff your traffic — the Nitro card drops packets that don't match the VPC ID.

🔒
LAYER 2: Hypervisor (KVM)

Strict ENI-to-VM mappings enforced at the hypervisor level. Each Elastic Network Interface is bound to exactly one VM. No cross-talk possible — a VM cannot receive traffic destined for another VM's ENI, even on the same host.

🗺️
LAYER 3: Mapping Service

A global SDN controller tracks every VPC→Customer→Host mapping across all AWS regions. Every Nitro card queries this service: "Where does this encapsulated packet go?" The mapping service is the authoritative source of truth for the entire AWS network fabric.

🔗 WHY THIS MATTERS — It Enables Three Critical Services

🔗
VPC Peering

Two VPCs share routes directly — no physical connection needed. The Nitro encapsulation handles routing between VPCs transparently. Traffic stays on the AWS backbone.

🌉
Transit Gateway

A cloud router connecting 1,000s of VPCs, VPNs, and Direct Connect. All traffic flows through the Nitro encapsulation layer — no physical network reconfiguration.

🔌
PrivateLink / VPC Endpoints

Traffic to AWS services (S3, DynamoDB, ECR) stays on the AWS backbone. Never touches the public internet. Powered by the same Nitro encapsulation.

🔄 STATEFUL vs STATELESS — The SG/NACL Distinction

✅ Security Groups (Stateful)

Security Groups track connections. If you allow inbound :80, the return traffic on ephemeral ports is automatically allowed. No need to open outbound ephemeral ranges. This is why you rarely touch NACLs — SGs handle 99% of use cases.

Allow inbound :80 → return traffic auto-allowed ✅

⚠️ NACLs (Stateless)

NACLs do NOT track connections. You must explicitly allow BOTH directions: inbound on port 80 AND outbound on ephemeral ports (1024-65535). Forgetting the outbound rule is the #1 NACL misconfiguration.

Allow inbound :80 + outbound 1024-65535 😓

📦 THE ACTUAL PACKET FLOW — User → anihpj Pod

1
DNS Resolution

User types anihpj.example.com → Route 53 resolves to the ALB's public IP in the public subnet.

2
Internet Gateway

Packet arrives at the VPC's Internet Gateway → routed to the ALB in the public subnet.

3
TLS Termination

ALB terminates TLS, inspects the request (host/path), and forwards to the Target Group — a list of Pod IPs on worker nodes in private subnets.

4
Nitro Encapsulation

The Nitro card encapsulates the packet: "VPC-abc, target 10.0.16.45:8000". This encapsulation guarantees the packet can only reach its intended VPC and host.

5
Mapping Service Route

The encapsulated packet traverses the AWS backbone. The mapping service tells each hop: "VPC-abc lives on host X in AZ-a" → packet forwarded accordingly.

6
Pod Delivery

Worker node's Nitro card receives the packet, strips encapsulation, delivers to Pod 10.0.16.45:8000. The anihpj container processes the request and responds.

⚡ Latency Profile

Same AZ: ~2-5ms  |  Cross-AZ: ~10ms  |  Cross-Region: varies by distance. The packet NEVER touches the public internet after step 2 — all intra-VPC traffic flows over the AWS backbone via Nitro encapsulation. This is what makes VPC networking both secure AND fast.

🌐
CIDR Blocks

IP range in CIDR notation: 10.0.0.0/16 = 65,536 IPs. Subnets split this: /24 = 256 IPs each.

🚪
Internet Gateway

Allows resources with public IPs to reach the internet. Attached to VPC. Public subnets route 0.0.0.0/0 → IGW.

🔒
NAT Gateway

Allows PRIVATE resources to reach the internet (pull Docker images, updates) WITHOUT being reachable FROM the internet. Deployed in public subnet.

🛡️
Security Groups

Stateful firewall at the instance/ENI level. Allow inbound → return traffic auto-allowed. Default: deny all inbound, allow all outbound.

✅ Security Groups (Stateful)

Rules specify ALLOW only. If you allow inbound port 80, return traffic on ephemeral ports is automatically allowed. Attached to ENI/instance.

⚠️ NACLs (Stateless)

Rules specify ALLOW and DENY. Must explicitly allow BOTH inbound AND outbound (return traffic on ephemeral ports 1024-65535). Attached to subnet.

9.2 VPC Design Patterns for EKS

EKS requires careful VPC design. The recommended pattern: Public + Private subnets across 3 Availability Zones. EKS worker nodes go in private subnets. The control plane endpoint can be public, private, or both.

EKS VPC Layout — Recommended Architecture
┌─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
│  VPC: 10.0.0.0/16 — EKS CLUSTER                                      │
│                                                                      │
│  ┌───────────────────────┐  ┌───────────────────────┐               │
│  │ us-east-1a            │  │ us-east-1b            │  us-east-1c   │
│  │ ┌───────────────────┐ │  │ ┌───────────────────┐ │  (same)       │
│  │ │ PUBLIC 10.0.0.0/24│ │  │ │ PUBLIC 10.0.1.0/24│ │              │
│  │ │ • NAT Gateway     │ │  │ │ • NAT Gateway     │ │              │
│  │ │ • Bastion Host    │ │  │ │                   │ │              │
│  │ └───────────────────┘ │  │ └───────────────────┘ │              │
│  │ ┌───────────────────┐ │  │ ┌───────────────────┐ │              │
│  │ │ PRIVATE 10.0.16.0 │ │  │ │ PRIVATE 10.0.17.0 │ │              │
│  │ │      /20          │ │  │ │      /20          │ │              │
│  │ │ • EKS Worker Nodes│ │  │ │ • EKS Worker Nodes│ │              │
│  │ │ • RDS Database    │ │  │ │ • ElastiCache     │ │              │
│  │ │ • Pods (via CNI)  │ │  │ │ • Pods (via CNI)  │ │              │
│  │ └───────────────────┘ │  │ └───────────────────┘ │              │
│  └───────────────────────┘  └───────────────────────┘               │
│                                                                      │
│  EKS Control Plane (AWS-managed account):                            │
│    • Endpoint: Private (recommended for production)                  │
│    • Worker nodes reach control plane via VPC endpoint               │
│    • kubectl reaches API via VPN/Direct Connect/bastion              │
│    • or: Public + Private endpoint for simpler access                │
└─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘
💡 VPC CNI Plugin — Pods Get Real VPC IPs

Unlike other K8s distributions that use overlay networks, EKS uses the AWS VPC CNI plugin. Every Pod gets a real, routable VPC IP address from the subnet's CIDR range. This means: Pods are directly addressable from other VPC resources, Security Groups work on Pods, and VPC Flow Logs show Pod traffic. The trade-off: each Pod consumes a VPC IP — plan your subnet sizes accordingly.

📊 DEEP DIVE: VPC Flow Logs — Network Monitoring

VPC Flow Logs capture IP traffic metadata flowing through your VPC. Think of it as "NetFlow for AWS" — you see WHO talked to WHOM, on which port, and whether it was accepted or rejected.

Flow Log Record Format — Captured per ENI, Subnet, or VPC
version account-id interface-id srcaddr dstaddr srcport dstport protocol packets bytes start end action log-status

# Example: anihpj Pod (10.0.16.45) accessing RDS (10.0.16.100) on port 5432
2 123456789 eni-abc123 10.0.16.45 10.0.16.100 45678 5432 6 15 2048 1623456789 1623456890 ACCEPT OK
📊
CloudWatch Logs

Real-time streaming + Metric Filters for alarms. Alert on REJECT records (blocked traffic). Best for: operational monitoring and troubleshooting.

🪣
S3

Long-term archive with Athena for SQL queries. Query years of flow data: "Show me all connections to port 22 last month." Best for: compliance auditing.

🔥
Kinesis Data Firehose

Real-time streaming to Splunk, ELK, Datadog. Correlate flow logs with application logs. Best for: SIEM integration and anomaly detection.

🔍
Troubleshoot Connectivity

Is traffic being blocked by a Security Group or NACL? Filter for action: REJECT — the flow log tells you exactly which rule blocked it.

🚨
Detect Anomalies

Spot unusual patterns: a Pod suddenly connecting to a foreign IP on port 22, or a spike in rejected traffic indicating a misconfigured SG.

📋
Compliance Auditing

Prove to auditors: "Only the application tier accessed the database tier on port 5432. No unauthorized access occurred."

🎯 EXAM TIPS
  • Flow Logs capture metadata only — not packet payloads. For payload inspection, use a Gateway Load Balancer with virtual appliance.
  • REJECT records = blocked by Security Group or NACL. ACCEPT = allowed through.
  • Published to CloudWatch OR S3, not both simultaneously from the same flow log. Use Kinesis if you need multiple destinations.
🔌 DEEP DIVE: VPC Endpoints — Keep Traffic Off the Internet

VPC Endpoints let AWS services communicate WITHOUT going through the public internet. Two types — know the difference for the exam:

🚪 GATEWAY ENDPOINT

FREE — S3 + DynamoDB only

Private Subnet ──▶ vpce-xxxx ──▶ S3/DynamoDB
via route table entry          private link
  • Add a route table entry in your subnet: destination = S3 prefix list, target = gateway endpoint ID.
  • No ENI created. Works at the route table level — any resource in the subnet can use it.
  • Traffic NEVER leaves the AWS network. No NAT Gateway needed, no internet exposure.
🔌 INTERFACE ENDPOINT

$0.01/hr + data — 100+ services

EKS Pod ──▶ ENI (private IP) ──▶ ECR/STS/CW
DNS: *.dkr.ecr      PrivateLink    
  • Creates an ENI with a private IP in your subnet. Uses PrivateLink — traffic stays on the AWS backbone.
  • DNS resolution automatically redirects service endpoints (e.g., *.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com) to the ENI's private IP.
  • Supports security groups — you can restrict which resources can access the endpoint.
☸️ Essential EKS VPC Endpoints

For a fully private EKS cluster (no NAT Gateway), you need these endpoints:

📦
ECR (2 endpoints)

com.amazonaws.region.ecr.dkr (image pull) + ecr.api (repository metadata). Without these, Pods can't pull images.

🪣
S3 (Gateway)

For ECR's backing S3 layer image storage. Gateway endpoint — free, route table entry only.

🔐
STS + CloudWatch

STS: for IRSA token exchange. CloudWatch: for container logs. Both are interface endpoints.

🎯 EXAM TIPS
  • Gateway endpoints = route table entry (FREE). Only for S3 and DynamoDB.
  • Interface endpoints = ENI + DNS redirect ($0.01/hr + data). For everything else (100+ services).
  • Both keep traffic within the AWS network — no NAT Gateway, no internet gateway, no public exposure.
  • If a question asks "how to access S3 from private subnet without NAT Gateway?" → Gateway Endpoint (free).

9.3 Load Balancing on AWS — ALB vs NLB

7️⃣Layer 7 — ALBHTTP/HTTPS/gRPC. Path-based, host-based routing. WAF integration.Web AppsEKS IngressMicroservices
4️⃣Layer 4 — NLBTCP/UDP/TLS. Ultra-low latency, static IPs, TLS termination.Game ServersIoTStreaming
3️⃣Layer 3 — GWLBIP packets. Routes through virtual appliances for inspection.FirewallsIDS/IPSDeep Packet Inspect
FeatureALB (Application LB)NLB (Network LB)
OSI LayerLayer 7 (HTTP/HTTPS/gRPC)Layer 4 (TCP/UDP/TLS)
RoutingPath-based, host-based, header-basedIP + Port only
Performance~100ms overhead, good for web appsUltra-low latency, millions of RPS
Static IP❌ (DNS name only)✅ Fixed IP per AZ
TLS Termination✅ Native✅ Native
WebSockets✅✅ (TCP-level)
Best ForWeb apps, microservices, EKS IngressHigh-throughput, static IPs, non-HTTP
🔀
GWLB (Gateway Load Balancer)

Layer 3 (IP). Routes traffic to virtual appliances (firewalls, IDS/IPS) for inspection BEFORE reaching your app. Uses GENEVE protocol. Transparent — preserves source/destination IPs.

⚠️
CLB (Classic Load Balancer)

Legacy. Layer 4 + basic Layer 7. AVOID for new deployments. AWS recommends ALB or NLB for all new workloads. Only appears in exam for migration scenarios.

ALB → EKS Pods via AWS Load Balancer Controller

└─────────────────────────────────────────────┘

9.4 Route 53 — DNS & Traffic Management

Route 53 is AWS's managed DNS service. It translates domain names (anihpj.example.com) into IP addresses (ALB endpoint). For DevOps, Route 53's routing policies enable advanced deployment patterns.

PolicyHow It WorksDevOps Use Case
📍Simple RoutingOne domain → one target. Basic DNS resolution with no health checks. The default policy.Single-region, non-critical endpoints. Quick prototyping.
⚖️Weighted RoutingSplit traffic by assigned weights: 90% → v1, 10% → v2. Weights must sum to any total — Route 53 normalizes them.Canary deployments! Shift 10% of users to new version, monitor error rates, then shift 100%. Also used for A/B testing.
⏱️Latency RoutingRoutes users to the AWS region with the lowest measured latency from their location. Uses live latency measurements, not geography.Multi-region active-active deployments. US users → us-east-1, EU users → eu-west-1, each with lowest latency.
🔄Failover RoutingActive-Passive: Primary target is healthy → all traffic goes there. Health check fails → auto-switch to secondary. Requires health checks on both.Disaster recovery. Primary region fails → Route 53 automatically redirects to DR region. Combined with cross-region replication.
🌍Geolocation RoutingRoute based on user's geographic location (continent, country, or US state). EU users → EU deployment, US users → US deployment.Compliance (GDPR: EU user data stays in EU). Localized content. Different pricing/features per region.
🔗Alias RecordsAWS-specific — points to AWS resources (ALB, CloudFront, S3 website, Elastic Beanstalk) for FREE. Resolves natively at the DNS level. Supports health checks.Always use Alias over CNAME for AWS resources. CNAME charges per query, Alias is free. Alias works at the zone apex (naked domain), CNAME does not.
🎯 EXAM TIP

Weighted Routing is the key policy for canary deployments and A/B testing on DOP-C02. Failover Routing is the key policy for disaster recovery (active-passive). Latency + Geolocation are often confused — latency is based on network performance, geolocation is based on the user's declared location. Alias records are ALWAYS the answer when routing to AWS resources like ALB, CloudFront, or S3 — they're free, support health checks, and work at zone apex.

9.5 CloudFront — Content Delivery Network

CloudFront caches your content at 600+ edge locations worldwide. Users get responses from the nearest edge, reducing latency from 200ms (cross-continent) to 10-20ms. For anihpj, CloudFront serves static files (CSS, JS, images) from S3 and routes dynamic requests to the ALB.

📁 Static Content (S3 + CloudFront)

CSS, JavaScript, images, user-uploaded files. Cached at edge locations with long TTL. Origin: S3 bucket with OAC.

⚡ Dynamic Content (ALB + EKS)

API calls, job post data, user authentication. NOT cached (or short TTL). Origin: ALB → EKS Pods.

🌐 DEEP DIVE: Lambda@Edge, CloudFront Functions & Cache Management

CloudFront has TWO serverless compute options at the edge — understanding when to use each is a DOP-C02 tested topic:

🧠 Lambda@Edge

More powerful, more latency, more triggers

  • Runs at Regional Edge Caches (13 locations, not all 600+ POPs)
  • Full Node.js / Python runtime — up to 30 seconds execution
  • 4 trigger points: Viewer Request, Origin Request, Origin Response, Viewer Response
  • Use cases: A/B testing (rewrite URLs), custom authentication at edge, dynamic content generation, header manipulation
⚡ CloudFront Functions

Lighter, faster, cheaper — 600+ locations

  • Runs at ALL 600+ edge locations — sub-millisecond startup
  • JavaScript only, lightweight (< 2ms execution time)
  • 2 trigger points only: Viewer Request, Viewer Response
  • Use cases: URL redirects, header manipulation, cache key normalization
  • ~1/6 the cost of Lambda@Edge — use when you don't need full runtime
🔐 OAC (Origin Access Control) — Replaces OAI

Ensures S3 objects can ONLY be accessed via CloudFront. Unlike the legacy OAI (which used S3 bucket policies), OAC uses IAM-based principal authentication. Users who try to hit the S3 bucket URL directly get a 403 Forbidden. Always use OAC for new CloudFront distributions — OAI is legacy.

Cache Invalidation — Purge Objects Before TTL Expires
# Invalidate specific files
aws cloudfront create-invalidation \
  --distribution-id E1ABCXYZ \
  --paths "/index.html" "/static/css/main.css"

# Invalidate everything (use sparingly — expensive at scale)
aws cloudfront create-invalidation \
  --distribution-id E1ABCXYZ \
  --paths "/*"

# Pricing: First 1,000 paths/month FREE, then $0.005 per path
⚠️ Cache Invalidation Reality

Invalidations are NOT instant — they can take a few minutes to propagate globally across all 600+ edge locations. For time-sensitive deployments, use versioned filenames instead (main.abc123.css) — this is instant because it's a new URL, not a cache purge. Invalidation is for emergencies; versioned filenames are for deployments.

9.6 API Gateway — Managed API Management

API Gateway is a fully managed service for creating, publishing, and securing APIs. It handles authentication, rate limiting, request transformation, and can integrate with Lambda, EKS, or any HTTP backend.

🔌
REST API

Full-featured: request validation, usage plans, API keys, caching. Best for public APIs.

⚡
HTTP API

Lightweight, cheaper (70% less), lower latency. Best for internal APIs, Lambda proxies.

🔗
VPC Link

Connects API Gateway to private resources inside VPC (EKS, internal ALB). No public exposure needed.

🌐ClientHTTPS Request

📊DynamoDBData Store
