What is the primary benefit of using immutable infrastructure in a CI/CD pipeline?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Consistency and reliability. Immutable infrastructure means you never patch running instances — you build a new AMI/container image and deploy fresh. This eliminates configuration drift, ensures the exact same artifact that passed tests reaches production, and makes rollback trivial (deploy the previous artifact).

Q6

In CodePipeline, what is the purpose of the S3 artifact bucket?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The artifact bucket passes files between pipeline stages. Each action's output artifacts are stored in S3 (encrypted with KMS) and become the input artifacts for the next stage. This decouples stages — the Build stage doesn't need to know where the Deploy stage is.

Q7

A pipeline has stages: Source → Build → Deploy-Staging → Manual-Approval → Deploy-Prod. The team wants to deploy to production only on weekdays between 9 AM and 5 PM. How can this be enforced?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Use a manual approval action with a CloudWatch Events rule. The approval action pauses the pipeline. A CloudWatch Events rule triggers a Lambda function that checks the current time — if outside business hours, the Lambda automatically rejects the approval. Alternatively, use a disabled transition between stages that a scheduled Lambda enables/disables.

Q8

What is the key difference between artifact promotion and rebuilding between environments?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Artifact promotion uses the EXACT same artifact (Docker image, AMI) across all environments. Rebuilding creates a new artifact per environment, which risks inconsistency — the staging-tested artifact may differ from the production artifact due to different build environments, dependency versions, or timing.

📖 EXPLANATION

Artifact promotion is a CI/CD best practice. The Docker image tagged anihpj:abc123 that passed all tests in staging is the EXACT same image pushed to production. No rebuild, no retag, no chance of drift. This is why ECR image tags should be immutable (git SHA-based) rather than mutable (:latest).

Q9

A canary deployment for anihpj shifts 10% traffic to the new version. After 5 minutes, CloudWatch shows the error rate is 8% (threshold is 5%). What happens?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeDeploy automatically rolls back by shifting traffic back to the previous version. The CloudWatch alarm on Errors > 5% triggers the rollback. This prevents a bad deployment from affecting more than 10% of users.

📖 EXPLANATION
# CloudWatch Alarm Configuration:
# Metric:   anihpj/Errors (sum per 1 min)
# Threshold: > 5 for 2 consecutive datapoints
# Action:    Trigger CodeDeploy rollback
# Result:    Traffic shifts back to previous version (0% → new, 100% → old)
#
# This is the power of canary deployments:
# blast radius limited to 10% of users
Q10

A DevOps engineer wants to ensure every deployment to EKS production follows the same auditable process. Which combination of AWS services provides the most complete solution?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodePipeline + CloudTrail + SNS. CodePipeline orchestrates the deployment with defined stages and manual approvals. CloudTrail logs every pipeline action (who approved, when, which revision). SNS notifies the team of pipeline state changes. Together they provide a fully auditable deployment process.

Chapter 13

13.1 CodeCommit — Git on AWS

AWS CodeCommit is a fully managed Git repository service. It's like GitHub, but hosted entirely within your AWS account — encrypted at rest, IAM-authenticated, and deeply integrated with CodePipeline:

🔐
IAM-Native Authentication

No personal access tokens. Use Git credentials (HTTPS) or SSH keys uploaded to IAM users. Every git push is authenticated via IAM — CloudTrail logs every operation.

🛡️
Encryption & Compliance

Repositories are encrypted at rest using KMS (customer-managed or AWS-managed keys). Encryption in transit via HTTPS or SSH. HIPAA, PCI, FedRAMP compliant.

🔄
PR Workflows & Branch Policies

Pull requests with approvals, branch protection rules, and triggers for CodePipeline. Require N approvals before merge. Notify SNS on PR creation.

CodeCommit vs Alternatives
  • CodeCommit: IAM-native, no external service. Best for: pure AWS shops, compliance-heavy environments.
  • GitHub: Larger ecosystem, Actions CI/CD. Best for: open-source, community collaboration.
  • GitLab: Built-in CI/CD, container registry. Best for: self-hosted needs, all-in-one platform.
  • Bitbucket: Jira integration, Atlassian ecosystem. Best for: teams already on Jira/Confluence.
Setting Up CodeCommit for anihpj
# Create repository
aws codecommit create-repository \
  --repository-name anihpj \
  --repository-description "Django job board app"

# Clone and push
git clone https://git-codecommit.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/v1/repos/anihpj
cd anihpj
git add . && git commit -m "Initial commit"
git push origin main

# Configure Git credential helper
git config --global credential.helper '!aws codecommit credential-helper $@'
git config --global credential.UseHttpPath true

13.2 CodeBuild — Managed Build Service

AWS CodeBuild is a fully managed build service that compiles source code, runs tests, and produces deployable artifacts. It scales automatically — you never manage build servers:

⚡
Auto-Scaling

Zero build server management. CodeBuild provisions compute resources on demand. Multiple builds run concurrently. Pay per build-minute.

🐋
Docker-Native

Every build runs in a Docker container. Use AWS-managed images (Amazon Linux 2, Ubuntu, Windows) or bring your own custom image from ECR/Docker Hub.

📦
Artifacts & Cache

Output artifacts pushed to S3. Build caching via S3 or local cache. Cache dependencies (pip, npm, maven) between builds for 10× speedup.

🔌
VPC Integration

Run builds inside your VPC. Access private resources: RDS for integration tests, private ECR, internal package registries.

buildspec.yml — anihpj Complete Build
version: 0.2

env:
  variables:
    IMAGE_REPO: "123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj"
    IMAGE_TAG: "latest"
  secrets-manager:
    DOCKER_HUB_PASS: "dockerhub-credentials:password"

phases:
  install:
    runtime-versions:
      python: 3.12
    commands:
      - pip install --upgrade pip
      - pip install -r requirements.txt
      - pip install bandit safety  # SAST tools

  pre_build:
    commands:
      - echo "Logging into ECR..."
      - aws ecr get-login-password --region $AWS_DEFAULT_REGION | docker login --username AWS --password-stdin $IMAGE_REPO
      - COMMIT_HASH=$(echo $CODEBUILD_RESOLVED_SOURCE_VERSION | cut -c 1-7)
      - echo "Running security scans..."
      - bandit -r . -f json -o bandit-report.json
      - safety check --json > safety-report.json

  build:
    commands:
      - echo "Running unit tests..."
      - python manage.py test --noinput --verbosity=2
      - echo "Building Docker image..."
      - docker build -t $IMAGE_REPO:$COMMIT_HASH -t $IMAGE_REPO:latest .
      - echo "Running container structure tests..."
      - docker run --rm $IMAGE_REPO:$COMMIT_HASH python -c "import django; print(django.VERSION)"

  post_build:
    commands:
      - echo "Pushing to ECR..."
      - docker push $IMAGE_REPO:$COMMIT_HASH
      - docker push $IMAGE_REPO:latest
      - echo "Creating artifact file..."
      - printf '{"ImageURI":"%s:%s"}' $IMAGE_REPO $COMMIT_HASH > imageDetail.json

artifacts:
  files:
    - imageDetail.json
    - appspec.yml
    - taskdef.json
  secondary-artifacts:
    TestReports:
      files: 'test-reports/*'
    SecurityReports:
      files: ['bandit-report.json', 'safety-report.json']

cache:
  paths:
    - '/root/.cache/pip/**/*'
    - '/root/.local/lib/python3.12/**/*'

13.3 CodeArtifact — Package Registry

AWS CodeArtifact is a fully managed artifact repository for software packages. It supports Maven, npm, pip, NuGet, Swift — allowing you to store, publish, and share packages within your organization without external internet access:

🏢
Domains & Repositories

A domain is a collection of repositories. Repositories within a domain can have upstream connections to public registries (PyPI, npm, Maven Central). Packages flow from public → upstream → your repo automatically.

⬆️
Upstream Repositories

Connect to public registries as upstream sources. When a developer requests pip install django, CodeArtifact checks your repo first, then fetches from PyPI if not cached. All downloaded packages are cached in your repo permanently.

🔒
Cross-Account Sharing

Share repositories across AWS accounts using resource-based policies. Central DevSecOps team curates approved packages; application teams consume them. All access via IAM.

🔬 DEEP DIVE: The Package Resolution Flow

When you run pip install django against CodeArtifact, here's exactly what happens behind the scenes:

1
Client Request

pip sends a GET request to the CodeArtifact repository endpoint with the IAM authorization token in the URL. CodeArtifact authenticates the caller via IAM and checks repository read permissions.

2
Check Local Repository

CodeArtifact checks: "Is 'django' already cached in the anihpj-python repository?" If yes (installed before), return it immediately — no external call needed. This is why builds are faster after the first run.

3
Check Upstream Repositories

If not found locally, CodeArtifact traverses the upstream chain: anihpj-python → (any intermediate repos) → public upstream (PyPI). It checks each repo in order until it finds the package. This allows you to chain multiple internal repos (e.g., security-audited-python → anihpj-python).

4
Fetch from Public Registry

If not found anywhere internally, CodeArtifact downloads the package from PyPI (or npmjs.com, Maven Central). The package is automatically cached in your repository — permanently. Next time someone asks for it, step 2 finds it and returns immediately.

5
Return to Client

CodeArtifact returns the package to pip. pip installs it. The entire resolution process is transparent — pip doesn't know or care whether the package came from cache, an upstream repo, or the public registry.

13.4 buildspec.yml for anihpj — Complete Walkthrough

Let's walk through the complete build process for anihpj, from git push to ECR image:

1. Source Download

CodePipeline triggers CodeBuild. CodeBuild clones the anihpj repository at the specified commit. Source files are placed in $CODEBUILD_SRC_DIR. The buildspec.yml at the repository root is read.

2. Install Phase

Python 3.12 runtime is provisioned. pip installs dependencies from requirements.txt plus SAST tools (bandit for Python code analysis, safety for known vulnerability checking). All dependencies cached for next build.

3. Pre-Build Phase

Docker logs into ECR using aws ecr get-login-password. The commit hash is extracted as the image tag (abc1234). Security scans run: bandit checks for code security issues, safety checks dependencies against known CVEs. This phase gates the pipeline — failures here block deployment.

4. Build Phase

Django unit tests execute (python manage.py test). If ALL tests pass, Docker builds the image using the multi-stage Dockerfile (from Chapter 3). Two tags applied: :abc1234 (immutable, git SHA) and :latest (movable). Container structurally tested with docker run --rm image python -c "import django".

5. Post-Build Phase

Both image tags are pushed to ECR. An artifact file (imageDetail.json) is created containing the full ECR image URI — this is passed to CodeDeploy in the next pipeline stage. Test reports and security reports are published as secondary artifacts.

📝 Chapter 13 — Exam Questions (CodeCommit, CodeBuild & CodeArtifact)

Q1

How does CodeCommit authenticate git operations differently from GitHub?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeCommit uses IAM-based authentication — Git credentials (HTTPS) or SSH keys uploaded to IAM users. GitHub uses personal access tokens or SSH keys managed outside AWS. Every CodeCommit operation is logged in CloudTrail automatically, providing a full audit trail of who pushed what and when.

Q2

In a CodeBuild buildspec.yml, what is the correct order of phases and what happens in each?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

install → pre_build → build → post_build. Install: set up runtime + dependencies. Pre-build: login to registries, set variables. Build: compile, test, build artifacts. Post-build: push artifacts, create output files.

Q3

The anihpj buildspec.yml needs to reference database credentials for integration tests. Where should these credentials be stored and how should buildspec reference them?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Store in AWS Secrets Manager. Reference in buildspec.yml via the env/secrets-manager section. Example: DB_PASSWORD: "anihpj-db-credentials:password". Never hardcode credentials in buildspec.yml — this is a security anti-pattern tested on DOP-C02.

Q4

What is the purpose of CodeArtifact domains and upstream repositories?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

A domain is a collection of repositories. An upstream repository connection allows a CodeArtifact repo to fetch packages from public registries (PyPI, npm, Maven Central) and cache them permanently. Once cached, packages remain available even if the public source removes them.

Q5

A CodeBuild project needs to access an RDS database in a private subnet for integration tests. How should this be configured?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Configure CodeBuild with VPC access — specify the VPC, private subnets, and security group. The build container gets a private IP in the VPC and can access private resources. Ensure the RDS security group allows inbound from the CodeBuild security group on the database port.

Q6

How can you speed up consecutive CodeBuild runs for the anihpj project?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Use build caching in buildspec.yml. Cache pip dependencies (/root/.cache/pip) and Python packages (/root/.local/lib/python3.12) between builds. Also use a custom Docker build image with pre-installed system dependencies to skip apt-get steps. Docker layer caching via --cache-from can also speed up image builds.

Q7

What is the difference between CodeCommit and S3 for storing source code?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeCommit is a Git repository with branches, pull requests, merge history, and diff views. S3 is object storage — you can store code there but you lose all Git features. CodePipeline can use S3 as a source provider for non-Git workflows (e.g., a Lambda drops a zip file in S3 to trigger a pipeline).

Q8

A developer reports that `pip install` from CodeArtifact fails with a 401 Unauthorized error. What is the most likely cause?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The CodeArtifact authorization token has expired. Tokens are valid for 12 hours. The solution: run aws codeartifact get-authorization-token to get a fresh token and update the pip config URL. In CodeBuild, automate this in the install phase.

Q9

What is the benefit of tagging Docker images with the git commit SHA instead of "latest"?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Immutability and traceability. A git SHA tag (anihpj:abc1234) uniquely identifies the exact commit that produced the image. You can trace any running container back to its source code. :latest is mutable — it changes with every build, making it impossible to know which version is running without checking the image digest.

Q10

A CodeBuild project needs to push images to ECR. What IAM permissions does the CodeBuild service role need?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The role needs: ecr:GetAuthorizationToken (for docker login), ecr:BatchCheckLayerAvailability, ecr:CompleteLayerUpload, ecr:InitiateLayerUpload, ecr:PutImage, ecr:UploadLayerPart. Also needs logs:CreateLogGroup, logs:CreateLogStream, logs:PutLogEvents for CloudWatch Logs, and S3 permissions for the artifact bucket.

Chapter 14

14.1 CodePipeline Architecture

AWS CodePipeline is a fully managed continuous delivery service that automates your release pipeline. It orchestrates the flow from source → build → test → deploy with visual workflow monitoring:

🔗
Pipeline Structure

A pipeline is composed of stages (logical groups like Source, Build, Deploy). Each stage contains actions (specific tasks like "pull from CodeCommit" or "deploy to EKS"). Actions within a stage can run sequentially or in parallel.

🔄
Transitions

Artifacts (files) pass between stages via S3 artifact bucket. Each action consumes input artifacts and produces output artifacts. The output of one action becomes the input of the next. Artifacts are encrypted with KMS.

🔌
Integration Ecosystem

Source: CodeCommit, GitHub, Bitbucket, S3, ECR. Build: CodeBuild, Jenkins. Deploy: CodeDeploy, CloudFormation, ECS, EKS, S3. Test: CodeBuild, third-party. Invoke: Lambda, Step Functions.

CloudFormation — anihpj CodePipeline
Resources:
  AnihpjPipeline:
    Type: AWS::CodePipeline::Pipeline
    Properties:
      Name: anihpj-pipeline
      RoleArn: !GetAtt PipelineRole.Arn
      ArtifactStore:
        Type: S3
        Location: !Ref ArtifactBucket
      Stages:
        - Name: Source
          Actions:
            - Name: CodeCommit_Source
              ActionTypeId: {Category: Source, Owner: AWS, Provider: CodeCommit, Version: "1"}
              Configuration:
                RepositoryName: anihpj
                BranchName: main
              OutputArtifacts: [{Name: SourceArtifact}]
        - Name: Build
          Actions:
            - Name: CodeBuild
              ActionTypeId: {Category: Build, Owner: AWS, Provider: CodeBuild, Version: "1"}
              Configuration:
                ProjectName: anihpj-build
              InputArtifacts: [{Name: SourceArtifact}]
              OutputArtifacts: [{Name: BuildArtifact}]
        - Name: Deploy-Dev
          Actions:
            - Name: DeployToEKSDev
              ActionTypeId: {Category: Deploy, Owner: AWS, Provider: CodeDeployToEKS, Version: "1"}
              Configuration:
                AppSpecTemplateArtifact: BuildArtifact
                TaskDefinitionTemplateArtifact: BuildArtifact

14.2 Stages, Actions & Transitions

Understanding how stages, actions, and transitions work together is critical for designing resilient pipelines:

ConceptWhat It IsKey Behavior
StageLogical group of related actions (Source, Build, Deploy)Stages execute sequentially. Stage 2 starts only after Stage 1 succeeds completely.
ActionIndividual task within a stageActions within a stage can run sequentially (default) or in parallel (set RunOrder).
TransitionEnabled/disabled gate between stagesDisable transition = pipeline stops at that stage. Useful for debugging or maintenance windows.
ArtifactFile bundle passed between stages via S3Encrypted with KMS. Max 1GB per artifact. Input artifacts are immutable during the action.
RevisionSpecific version of source (commit SHA)Each pipeline execution is tagged with the source revision it's processing.
ExecutionSingle run of the pipelineTriggered by source change, manual start, or CloudWatch Event. Has a unique execution ID.

14.3 Manual Approvals & Notifications

Manual approvals add a human gate to your pipeline — critical for compliance and regulated environments:

✋ Manual Approval Action
  • Inserts an approval step between stages (e.g., between Staging and Production).
  • Pipeline pauses until an authorized IAM user clicks Approve or Reject in the console/CLI.
  • Approval expires after 7 days (configurable). After expiry, the pipeline fails the stage.
  • SNS notification sent when approval is needed — integrates with Slack, email, PagerDuty.
  • Exam: Use manual approvals for production deployments. Combine with CloudWatch alarms for automated rollback if metrics degrade.
🔔 SNS Notification Integration
  • Create an SNS topic for pipeline events: arn:aws:sns:us-east-1:123456789:anihpj-pipeline-alerts.
  • Subscribe: email, SMS, Lambda, Slack webhook, PagerDuty.
  • CloudWatch Events rule triggers on pipeline state changes: STAGE_STARTED, ACTION_FAILED, APPROVAL_NEEDED.
  • Lambda function can parse the event and post a formatted message to Slack with approve/reject buttons.
  • Exam: CloudWatch Events + SNS = the standard pattern for pipeline notifications.

14.4 anihpj CI/CD Pipeline Design

Here's the complete pipeline architecture for anihpj, integrating all AWS CI/CD services:

📝CodeCommitgit push → main
→
🔨CodeBuildTest + Docker + ECR
→
☸️Deploy DevEKS rolling update
→
🧪Integration TestsRun against dev
→
✋Manual ApprovalSNS → Slack → Approve
→
🐤Prod Canary10%→25%→50%→100%
🎯 Pipeline Security Best Practices
  • IAM execution role: Pipeline gets its own IAM role with least-privilege permissions per stage. Source stage needs CodeCommit read; Build stage needs CodeBuild start; Deploy stage needs EKS write.
  • KMS encryption: Artifact bucket encrypted with customer-managed CMK. Cross-account pipelines share the key via KMS grant.
  • No secrets in source: All credentials from Secrets Manager or Parameter Store. buildspec references them via secrets-manager section — never hardcoded.
  • Pipeline execution history: CloudTrail logs every pipeline action. Audit who approved what deployment and when.
🧯 DEEP DIVE: Pipeline Error Handling & Retry Strategies

Pipelines fail — that's normal. What matters is how you handle failures. Here's the DOP-C02 tested approach:

🔄
Automatic Retry

CodePipeline automatically retries failed actions up to 3 times. If the action succeeds on retry, the pipeline continues normally. If all retries fail, the stage is marked as failed. Best practice: make build/test actions idempotent so retries are safe.

🔔
Failure Notification

Create a CloudWatch Events rule that matches ACTION_FAILED or STAGE_FAILED. Target an SNS topic → Lambda → Slack/PagerDuty. Include: pipeline name, stage, action, failure reason, and a link to the execution details in the notification.

🛑
Stop on Failure

Failed stages stop the pipeline. A disabled transition prevents automatic re-execution until you're ready. This is useful for: investigating the root cause, fixing the buildspec, or waiting for a dependency (e.g., "don't deploy until the database migration is complete").

📝 Chapter 14 — Exam Questions (CodePipeline)

Q1

What are the three core components of a CodePipeline stage?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Actions, input artifacts, and output artifacts. Each stage contains one or more actions (e.g., Source action, Build action). Actions consume input artifacts from the previous stage and produce output artifacts for the next stage via the S3 artifact bucket.

Q2

How can you run two actions in parallel within the same CodePipeline stage?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Set the RunOrder field to the same value for both actions. Actions with the same RunOrder execute in parallel. Actions with different RunOrder values execute sequentially (1 → 2 → 3). Example: run integration tests and security scans simultaneously after build.

Q3

A manual approval action is added between staging and production. How long can the pipeline wait for approval before failing?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

7 days (default, configurable). After the approval timeout expires, the pipeline fails the stage and the execution stops. SNS notifications are sent when approval is pending and when it expires. Shorter timeouts can be set for time-sensitive deployments.

Q4

How does CodePipeline integrate with external build systems like Jenkins?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Use the Jenkins action provider in CodePipeline. Register your Jenkins server with CodePipeline, then add a Build stage with the Jenkins provider. CodePipeline triggers the Jenkins job and waits for completion. Output artifacts from Jenkins are passed to the next pipeline stage.

Q5

A pipeline is triggered by a CodeCommit push. Where can you find the exact commit SHA that triggered the current execution?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

In the pipeline execution history in the CodePipeline console, or via the CODEBUILD_RESOLVED_SOURCE_VERSION environment variable available to CodeBuild actions. CloudTrail also logs the SourceRevision for every pipeline execution.

Q6

What is the purpose of a disabled transition in CodePipeline?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

It stops the pipeline at that stage. Useful for: maintenance windows (stop deployments while you upgrade the cluster), investigating failures without triggering new executions, or enforcing deployment freezes. The transition can be re-enabled via console, CLI, or SDK.

Q7

How should the CodePipeline service role be configured for least privilege?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Create a single IAM role for the pipeline with per-stage permissions: Source stage needs CodeCommit Get* only. Build stage needs CodeBuild StartBuild and S3 read/write. Deploy stage needs CodeDeploy CreateDeployment and EKS Describe*. No stage should have more permissions than it needs.

Q8

The anihpj pipeline deploys to EKS in three AWS accounts (dev, staging, prod). How should cross-account CodePipeline be implemented?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Pipeline runs in the tools account. Use cross-account IAM roles: the pipeline assumes a role in the target account for deployment. KMS key used for the artifact bucket must be shared via a KMS grant to the target account. S3 bucket policy must allow access from the target account.

Q9

What AWS service sends notifications when a pipeline execution fails?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CloudWatch Events + SNS. Create a CloudWatch Events rule that matches CodePipeline Action Execution State Change with state: FAILED. Target an SNS topic subscribed by email, Slack (via Lambda), or PagerDuty. This is the standard notification pattern for pipeline monitoring.

Q10

Can CodePipeline deploy to EKS directly, or is CodeDeploy required?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Both options exist. CodeDeploy for EKS provides blue/green and canary traffic shifting with automatic rollback. Alternatively, the CodePipeline ECS (Blue/Green) action or a CodeBuild action running kubectl can deploy directly. CodeDeploy is preferred for production because of its native traffic shifting and auto-rollback capabilities.

Chapter 15

15.1 CodeDeploy Components

AWS CodeDeploy automates application deployments to EC2, Lambda, and ECS/EKS. It handles the complex orchestration of stopping old versions, starting new ones, and shifting traffic:

📋
Application

The top-level container. Represents the software you're deploying. Example: anihpj. An application can have multiple deployment groups.

👥
Deployment Group

A set of target instances/revisions. Example: anihpj-prod (production EKS nodes), anihpj-staging (staging nodes). Tag-based or Auto Scaling group-based.

⚙️
Deployment Configuration

Defines HOW the deployment happens: AllAtOnce, HalfAtATime, OneAtATime, or custom (Canary, Linear). Controls speed and risk profile.

📄
AppSpec File

YAML/JSON file defining: what to deploy, where, and lifecycle hooks. appspec.yml for EC2/on-prem, different format for Lambda and ECS.

15.2 appspec.yml Deep Dive

The AppSpec file is the declarative deployment specification. It tells CodeDeploy exactly what to deploy and which lifecycle hooks to run:

appspec.yml — anihpj EKS Deployment
version: 0.0
Resources:
  - TargetService:
      Type: AWS::ECS::Service
      Properties:
        TaskDefinition: "arn:aws:ecs:us-east-1:123456789:task-definition/anihpj:1"
        LoadBalancerInfo:
          ContainerName: "anihpj"
          ContainerPort: 8000

# For EKS deployments, the AppSpec is simpler:
# version: 0.0
# Resources:
#   - TargetService:
#       Type: AWS::CodeDeploy::EKS
#       Properties:
#         TaskDefinition: <task-def-arn>
#         LoadBalancerInfo:
#           ContainerName: "anihpj"
#           ContainerPort: 8000

# EC2/On-Premise AppSpec includes hooks:
hooks:
  BeforeInstall: "scripts/stop_old.sh"
  AfterInstall: "scripts/set_permissions.sh"
  ApplicationStart: "scripts/start_anihpj.sh"
  ValidateService: "scripts/health_check.sh"
EC2 Lifecycle Hooks
  • BeforeBlockTraffic: Run before deregistering from LB
  • BlockTraffic: Deregister instance from LB
  • AfterBlockTraffic: Run after deregistration
  • ApplicationStop: Stop the old application
  • DownloadBundle: CodeDeploy copies files to instance
  • BeforeInstall: Pre-installation tasks
  • Install: Copy files to final location
  • AfterInstall: Post-install config
  • ApplicationStart: Start the new app
  • ValidateService: Health check
  • BeforeAllowTraffic: Pre-traffic validation
  • AllowTraffic: Register with LB
  • AfterAllowTraffic: Post-traffic tasks
Lambda/EKS Hooks
  • Lambda: Simpler — BeforeAllowTraffic, AfterAllowTraffic only. CodeDeploy shifts traffic between Lambda versions using weighted aliases.
  • EKS: Uses AWS::CodeDeploy::EKS resource type. CodeDeploy creates a new ReplicaSet with the new image, shifts traffic via ALB weighted target groups.
  • ECS: Blue/Green — creates replacement task set, shifts traffic, drains old tasks. Rollback is instant — just shift traffic back.

15.3 Deployment Groups & Configurations

ConfigBehaviorBest For
AllAtOnceDeploy to ALL instances simultaneously. Fastest, highest risk.Dev/Test environments
HalfAtATimeDeploy to 50% of instances, then remaining 50%. At least 50% always serving.General purpose
OneAtATimeDeploy to one instance at a time. Slowest, lowest risk per-instance.Small fleets, critical apps
Canary10Percent30Minutes10% traffic → wait 30 min → 100%High-traffic production
Linear10PercentEvery1Minute10% every minute until 100%Regulated, auditable
CustomDefine your own canary/linear percentages and intervalsSpecific compliance needs

15.4 Blue/Green Deployments for anihpj on EKS

Blue/Green on EKS uses ALB weighted target groups to shift traffic between two identical environments:

1. Create Green Environment

CodeDeploy creates a new Deployment (anihpj-green) in the same EKS namespace. The Green Deployment uses the new Docker image tag. Two ALB target groups exist: Blue (current prod) and Green (new version).

2. Test Green

Green Pods are running but receive 0% traffic initially. Integration tests and smoke tests run against the Green environment directly via port-forwarding or internal test endpoints.

3. Shift Traffic

ALB listener rule is updated: weight shifts from Blue=100% to Green=100%. Traffic shift is instantaneous at the ALB level — no DNS propagation delay. Users are now hitting the Green environment.

4. Monitor & Rollback (if needed)

CloudWatch monitors Green metrics for 5-60 minutes (configurable). If error rate or latency exceeds thresholds, auto-rollback: weight shifts back to Blue=100%. If healthy, Blue environment is terminated after the bake period.

🔄 DEEP DIVE: Rollback Strategies — Automatic vs Manual

Rollback is THE most critical part of any deployment strategy. If you can't rollback quickly, you can't deploy confidently. Here are the three rollback mechanisms you need to know for DOP-C02:

🤖
CodeDeploy Auto-Rollback

Configured in the deployment group: CloudWatch alarms on error rate, latency, or any custom metric. If ANY alarm triggers, CodeDeploy automatically shifts traffic back to the previous version. No human intervention needed. Best for: production deployments where response time matters more than investigation time.

👆
Manual Rollback (Console/CLI)

Stop the deployment in the CodeDeploy console or via aws deploy stop-deployment. Then create a new deployment targeting the previous revision. Best for: when you want to investigate the failure first, or when the rollback requires additional steps (database migration reversal).

☸️
Kubernetes Rollout Undo

kubectl rollout undo deployment/anihpj reverts to the previous ReplicaSet. Works with rolling updates. --to-revision=2 to go back to a specific revision. Best for: non-CodeDeploy-managed deployments, or quick rollbacks during development when you don't need traffic shifting.

⚠️ Rollback ≠ Undo Everything

Rollback reverts the application code to the previous version. It does NOT automatically reverse database migrations. If your deployment included a schema change, you need a separate rollback migration. This is why many teams use backward-compatible migrations: add columns/tables (don't drop anything), let the new version run, then clean up old schema in a separate deployment. This way, rollback doesn't require database rollback.

📝 Chapter 15 — Exam Questions (CodeDeploy)

Q1

What is the purpose of the AppSpec file in CodeDeploy?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The AppSpec file defines what to deploy, where, and how. For EC2: specifies source files, destination, and lifecycle hooks (BeforeInstall, ApplicationStart, etc.). For EKS: specifies the task definition and load balancer configuration for traffic shifting. For Lambda: specifies the function version and traffic shifting strategy.

Q2

Which CodeDeploy deployment configuration should be used for the FASTEST possible deployment with zero downtime?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeDeployDefault.AllAtOnce — deploys to all instances simultaneously. Fastest, but has the highest risk (no instances serving during the cutover moment). For zero-downtime requirements, use CodeDeployDefault.OneAtATime or HalfAtATime, or for EKS/Lambda use blue/green or canary.

Q3

During a Blue/Green deployment on EKS, how does CodeDeploy actually shift traffic?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeDeploy updates the ALB listener rule weights. The ALB has two target groups: Blue (current) and Green (new). CodeDeploy changes the weights from Blue=100/Green=0 to Blue=0/Green=100. The shift is instantaneous at the ALB level — no DNS propagation delay. Traffic shifts at the load balancer, not at DNS.

Q4

What is the difference between CodeDeploy's deployment group and deployment configuration?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Deployment Group: defines WHERE — which EC2 instances (by tags or ASG), Lambda functions, or EKS cluster. Deployment Configuration: defines HOW — the speed and strategy (AllAtOnce, OneAtATime, Canary10Percent30Minutes, etc.). They're separate concepts so you can reuse deployment configs across groups.

Q5

An EC2 deployment fails at the ApplicationStart hook. What happens to the instances?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The deployment fails and the instance is marked as failed. CodeDeploy stops the deployment to remaining instances (if using OneAtATime or HalfAtATime). Failed instances are NOT automatically rolled back unless auto-rollback is configured. Manual intervention or a new deployment is needed to recover failed instances.

Q6

How does CodeDeploy handle a canary deployment where the canary fails?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Automatic rollback — CodeDeploy shifts traffic back to the original version. The rollback is triggered by CloudWatch alarms on error rate, latency, or any custom metric. You configure which alarms to monitor in the deployment group. If ANY alarm triggers during the canary period, CodeDeploy rolls back automatically.

Q7

For an anihpj deployment to EKS, the developer wants to test the new Pods before ANY production traffic reaches them. Which AppSpec hook should be used?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Use a test listener on the ALB (separate from the production listener). Configure the test listener on a different port (e.g., 8443) that routes to the Green target group. Run tests against this endpoint. Only after tests pass does CodeDeploy shift production traffic to Green. The test listener is configured in the deployment group, not in AppSpec.

Q8

What is the rollback strategy when using `kubectl rollout undo` vs CodeDeploy auto-rollback?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

kubectl rollout undo: reverts the Deployment to the previous ReplicaSet. Rolling update in reverse. Works for standard K8s Deployments but doesn't coordinate with ALB traffic shifting. CodeDeploy auto-rollback: shifts ALB traffic back to the original target group. Instant at the LB level. Coordinated with CloudWatch monitoring. Preferred for production because it leaves the failed deployment intact for debugging.

Q9

A deployment group specifies tags to identify target EC2 instances. What happens if an instance has matching tags but is stopped?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeDeploy skips the stopped instance and marks it as skipped. The deployment continues to running instances. When the stopped instance starts later, it does NOT automatically receive the deployment. A new deployment would be needed, or use an Auto Scaling group as the deployment target — new instances launched by the ASG automatically get the latest deployment revision.

Q10

How can you ensure CodeDeploy deploys to new EC2 instances launched by an ASG?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Use the ASG as the deployment target (instead of tags). When the ASG launches new instances (scale-out or instance replacement), CodeDeploy automatically deploys the last successful revision. This ensures all instances in the ASG are running the same application version. This is the recommended pattern for auto-scaling environments.

Chapter 15B

15B.1 CodeArtifact — Package Management Deep Dive

CodeArtifact is AWS's fully managed package registry supporting Maven, npm, pip, NuGet, and Swift. It acts as a secure, private proxy to public registries:

🏗️
Domain → Repository Hierarchy

A domain (e.g., anihpj-artifacts) is the top-level container. It holds multiple repositories (e.g., anihpj-python for pip, anihpj-npm for frontend). Repositories within a domain can reference each other as upstream sources.

⬆️
Upstream Repository Flow

A repository can have upstream connections to public registries (PyPI, npmjs.com, Maven Central). When you request a package, CodeArtifact: (1) checks your repo, (2) checks upstream repos in your domain, (3) fetches from public registry if not cached. All downloaded packages are cached permanently — even if the public source deletes them.

Configuring pip for CodeArtifact
# Get authorization token (valid 12 hours)
aws codeartifact get-authorization-token \
  --domain anihpj-artifacts \
  --domain-owner 123456789 \
  --region us-east-1 \
  --query authorizationToken \
  --output text

# Configure pip to use CodeArtifact
pip config set global.index-url \
  https://aws:$CODEARTIFACT_TOKEN@anihpj-artifacts-123456789.d.codeartifact.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/pypi/anihpj-python/simple/

# Now pip install pulls from CodeArtifact (with PyPI upstream)
pip install django  # Cached in CodeArtifact after first pull

15B.2 EC2 Image Builder — Automated AMI Pipeline

EC2 Image Builder automates the creation, testing, and distribution of Golden AMIs. Instead of manually patching instances, you build a new AMI and redeploy:

1. Define Recipe

A recipe specifies: base image (Amazon Linux 2023, Ubuntu 22.04), components (build steps — install Docker, install kubelet, configure EKS bootstrap), and test components (verify Docker is installed, verify kubelet is running).

2. Build Pipeline

Image Builder launches a temporary EC2 instance, runs the build components in order, creates an AMI from the instance, runs test components against the AMI, then terminates the build instance. All automated — no manual steps.

3. Distribute & Share

The tested AMI is distributed to specified regions and/or shared with other AWS accounts. You can schedule pipelines: "Build a new Golden AMI every Monday at 2 AM with the latest security patches."

15B.3 AWS Amplify — Full-Stack CI/CD for Web Apps

AWS Amplify is a fully managed service for building, deploying, and hosting full-stack web applications with Git-based CI/CD:

🌐
Frontend Hosting

Amplify Hosting deploys React, Vue, Angular, Next.js apps to a global CDN. Connect to GitHub/GitLab/CodeCommit. Every git push triggers build + deploy. Feature branches get unique preview URLs.

⚡
Backend as Code

Define backends (Auth, API, Storage, Functions) in TypeScript using Amplify Gen 2. Backend is deployed as CloudFormation + CDK. Full-stack preview environments on every PR.

🔗
Amplify vs Alternatives

Amplify: Full-stack, Git-based, managed. Elastic Beanstalk: PaaS for traditional apps. S3+CloudFront: Static hosting only, simplest. Choose Amplify for modern full-stack JS apps with CI/CD built in.

📝 Chapter 15B — Exam Questions (CodeArtifact, Image Builder & Amplify)

Q1

How does CodeArtifact's upstream repository feature reduce external dependencies?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

CodeArtifact caches ALL packages fetched from upstream registries (PyPI, npm, Maven Central) permanently. Even if the public registry deletes a package or goes offline, your cached copy remains available. This prevents "left-pad" style incidents where a removed public package breaks all dependent builds.

Q2

What is the primary benefit of using EC2 Image Builder over manually creating AMIs?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Image Builder automates the entire AMI lifecycle: build → test → distribute. Manual AMI creation is error-prone (forgotten patches, inconsistent configurations). Image Builder ensures every AMI is built from a tested recipe, validated with test components, and distributed to all target regions automatically — on a schedule.

Q3

How should pip be configured to use CodeArtifact as its package index?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

pip config set global.index-url https://aws:$TOKEN@domain-owner.d.codeartifact.region.amazonaws.com/pypi/repo/simple/. The token is obtained via aws codeartifact get-authorization-token and is valid for 12 hours. In CodeBuild, this is automated in the install phase.

Q4

What is the difference between EC2 Image Builder components and recipes?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Components: individual build or test steps (e.g., "install Docker", "verify kubelet version"). A recipe combines a base image with a sequence of components. A recipe defines: base image (AMI), build components (what to install), test components (how to validate), and distribution settings (which regions/accounts).

Q5

How does AWS Amplify differ from S3 + CloudFront for hosting a React app?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Amplify provides Git-based CI/CD out of the box: push code → auto-build → deploy to CDN. Feature branches get unique preview URLs. S3+CloudFront is static hosting only — you must build and upload files manually (or via your own pipeline). Amplify also includes backend-as-code (Auth, API, Storage) that S3+CloudFront doesn't.

Q6

A security scan finds a vulnerability in a Python package cached in CodeArtifact. How should this be handled?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Dispose the vulnerable package version from CodeArtifact. This prevents any future builds from pulling it. Update the application to use the patched version. CodeArtifact automatically fetches the new version from the upstream (PyPI) and caches it. Use aws codeartifact dispose-package-versions to remove the vulnerable version.

Q7

What is a Golden AMI and how does Image Builder help maintain it?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

A Golden AMI is a pre-configured, security-hardened, tested AMI that serves as the standard for all EC2 instances. Image Builder automates Golden AMI maintenance: schedule weekly builds, apply latest patches, run validation tests, distribute to all regions. New instances always launch from the latest Golden AMI — never patch running instances (immutable infrastructure).

Q8

How can CodeArtifact be shared across multiple AWS accounts?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Use resource-based policies on the CodeArtifact repository. Grant codeartifact:ReadFromRepository to principals in other accounts. Cross-account consumers use the same pip/npm configuration with their own IAM credentials. The domain owner can also share the entire domain, giving access to all repositories within it.

Q9

An Image Builder pipeline fails at the test phase. What happens to the AMI?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

The AMI is NOT distributed. Image Builder terminates the build instance and marks the pipeline execution as failed. No AMI is shared or distributed to target regions. This is the safety gate — only tested, validated AMIs reach production. CloudWatch Events can notify on failure for investigation.

Q10

When would you choose AWS Amplify over Elastic Beanstalk for a web application?

Show Answer & Explanation
✅ ANSWER

Amplify: Modern full-stack JavaScript apps (React, Next.js, Vue) with Git-based CI/CD, serverless backends, and global CDN. Elastic Beanstalk: Traditional server-rendered apps (Django, Rails, Spring) that need an application server. Choose Amplify when your frontend is a SPA/SSR JS framework and your backend is serverless. Choose Beanstalk when you need a traditional web server with full OS access.

⏱️ 20 minutes
🏋️ Drill 12: Design a CI/CD Pipeline for anihpj

Your team wants to implement CI/CD for the anihpj Django application on AWS EKS. Requirements: (1) Automated builds on every git push, (2) Unit tests must pass before any deployment, (3) Deploy to dev automatically on merge to main, (4) Deploy to production requires manual approval, (5) Production deployment must be zero-downtime, (6) Automatic rollback if error rate exceeds 5%.

  1. Draw the pipeline stages and identify which AWS service handles each stage
  2. Write the deployment strategy for each environment (dev, staging, prod)
  3. Configure the rollback trigger in CloudWatch
💡 Hint: CodeCommit → CodeBuild (with buildspec.yml) → CodePipeline → CodeDeploy. Production = Blue/Green or Canary. CloudWatch alarm on Errors metric triggers auto-rollback.
▼ Solution (Click to Reveal) 
# PIPELINE DESIGN:
# Source: CodeCommit (main branch trigger)
# Build: CodeBuild (buildspec.yml: test + docker build + ECR push)
# Deploy-Dev: CodeDeploy → EKS (rolling update, auto)
# Deploy-Staging: CodeDeploy → EKS (Blue/Green, auto with test gate)
# Approval: Manual approval action (SNS → Slack)
# Deploy-Prod: CodeDeploy → EKS (Canary 10%→30min→100%)
#
# ROLLBACK: CloudWatch Alarm on anihpj/Errors > 5% for 2 datapoints
# → triggers Lambda → CodePipeline stop + CodeDeploy rollback
#
# DEPLOYMENT STRATEGIES:
# Dev: Rolling update (fast, acceptable brief overlap)
# Staging: Blue/Green (full test before traffic switch)
# Production: Canary (10% traffic → monitor → gradual rollout)
⏱️ 15 minutes
🏋️ Drill 13: Build anihpj with CodeBuild + CodeArtifact

Configure a CodeBuild project for anihpj that: (1) Uses a custom build image with Python 3.12 pre-installed, (2) Pulls Python dependencies from CodeArtifact instead of public PyPI, (3) Caches pip dependencies between builds, (4) Runs bandit and safety security scans in the build, (5) Tags the Docker image with the git commit SHA.

  1. Write the buildspec.yml with all phases
  2. Configure CodeArtifact domain and repository with PyPI upstream
  3. Write the pip config command to point to CodeArtifact
💡 Hint: Use env/secrets-manager for CodeArtifact token. Use cache/paths for pip cache. aws codeartifact get-authorization-token + pip config set global.index-url.
⏱️ 20 minutes
🏋️ Drill 14: Build Complete CI/CD Pipeline for anihpj

Implement an end-to-end CodePipeline for anihpj with these stages: Source (CodeCommit) → Build (CodeBuild) → Deploy-Dev (EKS) → Integration-Test (CodeBuild) → Manual-Approval → Deploy-Prod (Canary to EKS). The pipeline must: (1) Use KMS-encrypted S3 artifact bucket, (2) Notify Slack on approval needed, (3) Have CloudWatch alarms for canary monitoring.

  1. Design the pipeline stages and action providers
  2. Write the CloudFormation template for the pipeline
  3. Configure the SNS→Slack notification integration
💡 Hint: Pipeline artifact bucket must be versioned with KMS CMK. Each stage references InputArtifacts/OutputArtifacts. SNS topic subscribed by Lambda → Slack webhook.
⏱️ 15 minutes
🏋️ Drill 15: Blue/Green Deploy anihpj to EKS

Configure CodeDeploy to perform a Blue/Green deployment of anihpj to your EKS cluster. (1) Write the appspec.yml for EKS deployment, (2) Configure the ALB with two target groups (Blue and Green), (3) Set up the deployment to shift traffic from Blue to Green after 5 minutes of healthy metrics, (4) Configure automatic rollback if the Green deployment fails health checks.

  1. Write the appspec.yml with EKS resource type
  2. Configure the deployment group with the correct deployment configuration
  3. Set up the rollback trigger
💡 Hint: Use AWS::CodeDeploy::EKS in appspec.yml. ALB listener rule with weighted target groups. CodeDeploy deployment configuration = CodeDeployDefault.ECSLinear10PercentEvery1Minute or custom.
⏱️ 15 minutes
🏋️ Drill 15B: CodeArtifact for anihpj Dependencies + Golden AMI

Set up a secure package management pipeline: (1) Create a CodeArtifact domain and Python repository with PyPI upstream, (2) Configure CodeBuild to pull from CodeArtifact instead of public PyPI, (3) Create an EC2 Image Builder pipeline that builds a Golden AMI with Docker, kubelet, and the CodeArtifact pip config pre-installed for EKS worker nodes.

  1. Write the CodeArtifact repository configuration
  2. Modify buildspec.yml to authenticate with CodeArtifact
  3. Design the Image Builder recipe components
💡 Hint: Image Builder recipe = base image (AL2023) + build component (install Docker, kubelet) + test component (verify Docker version). Distribute to all EKS cluster regions.

🟡 Part 5: Infrastructure as Code on AWS

Exam Domain: Domain 2 — Configuration Management & IaC (17% of DOP-C02). IaC is the foundation of DevOps automation — every resource is defined as code, versioned in Git, and deployed consistently. This part covers CloudFormation, CDK, Terraform, Config, Systems Manager, Elastic Beanstalk, and SAM.

Chapter 16: CloudFormation — AWS Infrastructure as Code

CloudFormation is AWS's native Infrastructure as Code service. Write a YAML or JSON template describing every resource your application needs, and CloudFormation provisions them in dependency order — with automatic rollback on failure. Master CloudFormation and you master repeatable, version-controlled AWS infrastructure.

🟡 Intermediate ⏱️ ~4 hours 🎯 Domain 2: Config Mgmt 17% 📝 10 Questions

16.1 CloudFormation Fundamentals

AWS CloudFormation is the original AWS IaC service. You write a template (JSON or YAML) describing the resources you want, and CloudFormation provisions and configures them in the correct order, handling dependencies automatically.

🧠 Why Infrastructure as Code Matters

Before IaC, provisioning AWS resources meant clicking through the console — click-ops. This created three critical problems that IaC solves:

🔁
Reproducibility

Click-ops: "I think I set the security group this way... maybe?" With IaC, every resource is defined in code — deploy the same template twice, get identical infrastructure. Dev, staging, and production are byte-for-byte identical environments.

📝
Version Control

Infrastructure changes become Git commits with diffs, PRs, and reviews. "Who changed the RDS instance type?" → git blame tells you exactly who, when, and why. Rollback is git revert + redeploy — infrastructure history is your backup.

🚫
No Configuration Drift

Click-ops leads to drift: the console says one thing, another engineer changed it manually, and nobody knows what's actually running. IaC templates are the single source of truth — CloudFormation Drift Detection finds and reports any manual changes.

📝
Template

A JSON or YAML file that declaratively describes your infrastructure. The single source of truth — versioned in Git. No manual steps, no click-ops, no configuration drift over time.

📦
Stack

A collection of AWS resources created from a template. You create, update, and delete resources as a single unit — the stack. If stack creation fails, CloudFormation automatically rolls back (unless you disable it).

🔄
Change Set

A preview of changes before applying them. Shows exactly which resources will be added, modified, or deleted. Always review the change set before updating a production stack.

CloudFormation Template — anihpj S3 Bucket
AWSTemplateFormatVersion: "2010-09-09"
Description: "anihpj static files S3 bucket with versioning"

Parameters:
  Environment:
    Type: String
    AllowedValues: [dev, staging, prod]
    Default: dev

Resources:
  AnihpjStaticBucket:
    Type: AWS::S3::Bucket
    Properties:
      BucketName: !Sub "anihpj-static-${Environment}-${AWS::AccountId}"
      VersioningConfiguration:
        Status: Enabled
      PublicAccessBlockConfiguration:
        BlockPublicAcls: true
        BlockPublicPolicy: true
        IgnorePublicAcls: true
        RestrictPublicBuckets: true

Outputs:
  BucketName:
    Value: !Ref AnihpjStaticBucket
    Description: "anihpj static files bucket"

16.2 Templates, Stacks & StackSets

ConceptWhat It IsScopeUse Case
TemplateJSON/YAML file describing resourcesSingle file, versioned in GitDefine infrastructure as code
StackCollection of resources from a single templateSingle AWS Region, single accountDeploy an application environment
StackSetDeploy stacks across multiple accounts/regionsMulti-account, multi-regionSecurity baseline across entire Org
Nested StackStack referenced within another templateParent-child relationshipModular templates: VPC → EKS → anihpj
Change SetPreview of what a stack update will changeSingle stackReview before applying to production
🌐 DEEP DIVE: StackSets — Multi-Account, Multi-Region Governance

StackSets is CloudFormation's enterprise governance feature. Deploy and manage the same template across multiple AWS accounts and regions from a single administrator account:

🔐
Self-Managed Permissions

You create IAM roles in each target account manually. Administrator role assumes the execution role. Best for: cross-Org deployments or when you need fine-grained control over which accounts can be targeted.

🏢
Service-Managed Permissions

Integrates with AWS Organizations. StackSets automatically deploys to all accounts in an OU — including new accounts added later. Best for: security baseline, Config rules, IAM roles across entire Org.

Example: Deploy anihpj-security-baseline (CloudTrail, Config, GuardDuty) to all 50 accounts in the Org — update once, all accounts receive the change simultaneously.

🔧 DEEP DIVE: cfn-init — Bootstrapping EC2 Instances

cfn-init is CloudFormation's mechanism for configuring EC2 instances at launch. Unlike User Data (which runs once at first boot), cfn-init can detect template changes and reconfigure running instances:

Traditional User Data
  • Runs ONLY at first boot (via cloud-init)
  • Template updates don't reconfigure running instances
  • No built-in signaling — CloudFormation doesn't know when it's done
  • If User Data fails silently, CloudFormation thinks the instance is ready
cfn-init + cfn-signal
  • cfn-init reads Metadata from the CloudFormation stack — can re-run on updates
  • cfn-signal tells CloudFormation: "I'm ready" or "I failed"
  • CreationPolicy waits for cfn-signal before marking the resource CREATE_COMPLETE
  • If cfn-signal reports failure → stack rolls back — no partial deployments

16.3 Intrinsic Functions & Pseudo Parameters

CloudFormation provides powerful built-in functions for dynamic template construction:

🔗
!Ref

Returns the physical ID of a resource or the value of a parameter. !Ref AnihpjBucket → bucket name. Most commonly used function.

📝
!Sub

Substitutes variables into a string. !Sub "arn:aws:s3:::${Bucket}". Supports both inline and mapping syntax for complex substitutions.

🗺️
!FindInMap

Looks up values in the Mappings section. !FindInMap [RegionMap, !Ref "AWS::Region", "AMI"] — maps region → AMI ID.

🔀
!Select & !Join

!Select [0, !Split ["/", !Ref Path]] — pick from a list. !Join ["", ["prefix-", !Ref Name]] — concatenate strings.

🏷️
Pseudo Parameters

AWS::Region, AWS::AccountId, AWS::StackName, AWS::StackId, AWS::NoValue. Always available, never need to declare, auto-populated by CloudFormation.

🔐
Dynamic References

{{resolve:ssm:/anihpj/db-password:1}} — fetch secrets from SSM Parameter Store or Secrets Manager at deploy time. Never hardcode secrets in templates.

Intrinsic Functions — Real Examples
# !Ref — reference a resource or parameter
SecurityGroups: [!Ref ClusterSecurityGroup]

# !Sub — substitute variables inline
Name: !Sub "anihpj-${Environment}-${AWS::Region}"

# !Join — concatenate strings with delimiter
BucketPolicy: !Join ["", ["arn:aws:s3:::", !Ref BucketName]]

# !FindInMap — lookup from Mappings section
ImageId: !FindInMap [RegionMap, !Ref "AWS::Region", AMI]

# Dynamic Reference — resolve secrets at deploy time
MasterPassword: '{{resolve:secretsmanager:anihpj-db:SecretString:password}}'

16.4 CloudFormation for anihpj EKS

Here's the key resources needed to provision an EKS cluster with CloudFormation:

CloudFormation — EKS Cluster for anihpj
Resources:
  # IAM Role for EKS Cluster
  EKSClusterRole:
    Type: AWS::IAM::Role
    Properties:
      AssumeRolePolicyDocument:
        Statement:
          - Effect: Allow
            Principal: {Service: eks.amazonaws.com}
            Action: sts:AssumeRole
      ManagedPolicyArns:
        - arn:aws:iam::aws:policy/AmazonEKSClusterPolicy

  # EKS Cluster
  AnihpjCluster:
    Type: AWS::EKS::Cluster
    Properties:
      Name: anihpj-cluster
      RoleArn: !GetAtt EKSClusterRole.Arn
      ResourcesVpcConfig:
        SecurityGroupIds: [!Ref ClusterSG]
        SubnetIds: [!Ref PrivateSubnet1, !Ref PrivateSubnet2, !Ref PrivateSubnet3]
      Version: "1.29"

  # Managed Node Group
  AnihpjNodeGroup:
    Type: AWS::EKS::Nodegroup
    Properties:
      ClusterName: !Ref AnihpjCluster
      NodeRole: !GetAtt NodeInstanceRole.Arn
      Subnets: [!Ref PrivateSubnet1, !Ref PrivateSubnet2]
      ScalingConfig:
        MinSize: 2
        DesiredSize: 3
        MaxSize: 6
      InstanceTypes: [t3.medium]
🎯 CloudFormation Best Practices for DOP-C02
  • Use Parameters for environment-specific values (instance type, CIDR ranges). Never hardcode.
  • Use Outputs with Exports to share values between stacks (VPC ID → EKS stack).
  • Enable termination protection on production stacks to prevent accidental deletion.
  • Always review Change Sets before applying updates — they show exactly what will change.
  • StackSets + Organizations for deploying security baselines across all accounts automatically.
🧯 DEEP DIVE: Stack Failures & Rollback — What Actually Happens

CloudFormation has a sophisticated rollback system. Understanding it prevents production disasters:

🔄
CREATE_FAILED → ROLLBACK

Default behavior. If any resource fails during stack creation, CloudFormation deletes ALL resources it created and the stack ends in ROLLBACK_COMPLETE. The template remains — fix it and retry. Set OnFailure: DO_NOTHING to preserve resources for debugging.

⚠️
UPDATE_ROLLBACK_FAILED

The scariest stack status. Rollback itself failed — resources may be in an inconsistent state. Recovery: use ContinueUpdateRollback from the console/CLI to skip the problematic resource and continue rolling back others. This is why you test in dev first.

🛡️
Stack Policies

A JSON document that prevents updates to critical resources. Example: "nobody can modify the production RDS database via CloudFormation." Unlike termination protection (which prevents deletion), stack policies prevent modification — apply to databases, IAM roles, and VPC infrastructure.

📝 Chapter 16 — CloudFormation (10 Questions)

Q1

What happens by default when a CloudFormation stack creation fails?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Automatic rollback. All resources created up to the failure point are deleted. The stack status becomes ROLLBACK_COMPLETE. You can disable this behavior by setting OnFailure: DO_NOTHING to preserve resources for debugging.

Q2

What is a CloudFormation Change Set?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

A preview showing exactly which resources will be added, modified, or deleted before applying a stack update. Always review the Change Set in production — it prevents surprises like accidental resource replacement or deletion.

Q3

How do you reference a resource's physical ID in CloudFormation?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

!Ref LogicalResourceName. For most resources, this returns the physical ID (e.g., bucket name, instance ID). For parameters, it returns the parameter value. This is the most commonly used intrinsic function.

Q4

What is StackSets used for?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Multi-account, multi-region stack deployment. Deploy the same template across multiple AWS accounts and regions from a single operation. Common use case: deploy security baseline (IAM roles, Config rules) to all accounts in an AWS Organization.

Q5

How can you prevent accidental deletion of a production CloudFormation stack?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Enable termination protection on the stack. This prevents deletion via console, CLI, or API until protection is explicitly disabled. Also use DeletionPolicy: Retain on critical resources to preserve them even if the stack IS deleted.

Q6

What does !Sub do in CloudFormation?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Substitutes variables into strings. !Sub "arn:aws:s3:::${BucketName}" replaces ${BucketName} with the value of the parameter or resource reference. Supports both inline and mapping syntax for complex substitutions.

Q7

What is CloudFormation Drift Detection?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Detects when actual resource configurations differ from the stack template. Someone manually modified a resource (click-ops) — Drift Detection identifies the mismatch. Run it periodically to ensure your infrastructure matches your declared template.

Q8

How do you deploy a CloudFormation template with IAM resources?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Pass --capabilities CAPABILITY_IAM (for IAM resources) or CAPABILITY_NAMED_IAM (for named IAM resources). This is a safety mechanism — CloudFormation requires explicit acknowledgment when creating IAM resources that could grant permissions.

Q9

What is the difference between DependsOn and !Ref for resource ordering?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

!Ref creates an implicit dependency — CloudFormation automatically determines the correct order. DependsOn creates an explicit dependency when CloudFormation can't infer it (e.g., one resource needs another to exist but doesn't reference it directly).

Q10

How can CloudFormation pass secrets to EC2 instances without hardcoding them?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Use dynamic references: {{resolve:ssm:/anihpj/db-password:1}} or {{resolve:secretsmanager:anihpj-db:SecretString}}. CloudFormation resolves the value at deploy time from SSM Parameter Store or Secrets Manager without exposing it in the template.

⏱️ 20 min
🏋️ Drill 16: Provision EKS with CloudFormation

Write a CloudFormation template that provisions: VPC (3 AZs, public+private subnets), EKS cluster with managed node group, IAM roles for cluster and nodes. Apply it and verify the cluster with kubectl get nodes.

Chapter 17: AWS CDK — Infrastructure as REAL Code

The AWS Cloud Development Kit lets you define infrastructure using TypeScript, Python, Java, C#, or Go — with loops, conditionals, classes, and unit tests. CDK synthesizes your code into CloudFormation templates behind the scenes. Write infrastructure like you write application code.

🟡 Intermediate ⏱️ ~2 hours 🎯 Domain 2: Config Mgmt 17% 📝 10 Questions

17.1 CDK vs CloudFormation

AWS CDK (Cloud Development Kit) lets you define infrastructure using real programming languages — TypeScript, Python, Java, C#, Go. CDK synthesizes your code into a CloudFormation template behind the scenes:

📝 CloudFormation (YAML/JSON)
  • Declarative templates in markup language
  • Verbose — large templates get unwieldy
  • Limited abstractions (nested stacks, macros)
  • No IDE autocomplete for resource properties
  • No unit testing infrastructure code
  • Best for: simple, well-known architectures
💻 CDK (Python/TS/Java/C#/Go)
  • Real programming languages with logic, loops, classes
  • Higher-level L2/L3 Constructs with sensible defaults
  • IDE support — autocomplete, type checking, refactoring
  • Unit test infrastructure with pytest, jest, etc.
  • Reusable component libraries (Construct Hub)
  • Best for: complex architectures, multi-environment, teams

17.2 Constructs, Stacks & Apps

CDK organizes code in three levels. Every CDK app follows the same hierarchy:

🧱
L1 — Cfn* Constructs

Direct 1:1 mapping to CloudFormation resources. CfnBucket → AWS::S3::Bucket. All properties must be explicitly set. Verbose but complete control.

🏗️
L2 — Curated Constructs

Higher-level, opinionated wrappers. Bucket auto-generates a unique name, applies best-practice defaults (encryption, versioning). Use these by default.

📦
L3 — Patterns

Pre-built architectures. ApplicationLoadBalancedFargateService creates ALB + Fargate + task + security groups in one construct. Fastest path to production patterns.

🏗️ The CDK App → Stack → Construct Hierarchy
App (root container)
│ contains
Stack: Dev
Stack: Prod
│ each contains
Vpc Construct
Cluster Construct
NodeGroup Construct
↓ cdk synth
CloudFormation Template (JSON)

17.3 CDK for EKS — Python Example

Python CDK — EKS Cluster for anihpj
from aws_cdk import (
    Stack, aws_eks as eks, aws_ec2 as ec2, aws_iam as iam
)
from constructs import Construct

class AnihpjEksStack(Stack):
    def __init__(self, scope: Construct, id: str, **kwargs):
        super().__init__(scope, id, **kwargs)

        # VPC (assume already exists or create new)
        vpc = ec2.Vpc.from_lookup(self, "Vpc", vpc_name="anihpj-vpc")

        # EKS Cluster
        cluster = eks.Cluster(self, "AnihpjCluster",
            version=eks.KubernetesVersion.V1_29,
            vpc=vpc,
            default_capacity=0,  # Use managed node groups
            vpc_subnets=[ec2.SubnetSelection(subnet_type=ec2.SubnetType.PRIVATE_WITH_EGRESS)]
        )

        # Managed Node Group
        cluster.add_nodegroup_capacity("anihpj-nodes",
            instance_types=[ec2.InstanceType("t3.medium")],
            min_size=2, desired_size=3, max_size=6
        )

        # Deploy anihpj from manifest
        cluster.add_manifest("anihpj-deploy", {
            "apiVersion": "apps/v1", "kind": "Deployment",
            "metadata": {"name": "anihpj", "labels": {"app": "anihpj"}},
            "spec": {
                "replicas": 3,
                "selector": {"matchLabels": {"app": "anihpj"}},
                "template": {
                    "metadata": {"labels": {"app": "anihpj"}},
                    "spec": {"containers": [{
                        "name": "anihpj",
                        "image": "123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1",
                        "ports": [{"containerPort": 8000}]
                    }]}
                }
            }
        })
🧪 DEEP DIVE: CDK Best Practices for Production
🧪
Unit Test Infrastructure

Use cdk.assertions.Template (Python). Validate: "Does the synthesized template have an S3 bucket with encryption enabled?" Catch misconfigurations before deployment. Run in CI/CD as a pre-deploy gate.

🔄
CDK Pipelines

Self-mutating CI/CD: define your pipeline in CDK code. Push changes → pipeline updates ITSELF first → then deploys infrastructure. Multi-environment bootstrapping: dev→staging→prod with manual approval gates.

🛡️
CDK Aspects & CDK Nag

Aspects: visit every construct — add encryption, block public access, enforce tagging. CDK Nag: AWS Solutions rules (PCI, HIPAA, NIST). Catch security violations at synth time.

📝 Chapter 17 — AWS CDK (10 Questions)

Q1

What is the relationship between CDK and CloudFormation?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

CDK synthesizes code into CloudFormation templates. cdk synth generates a JSON/YAML template. cdk deploy submits it to CloudFormation. CDK is a higher-level abstraction — it writes CloudFormation for you.

Q2

What are L1, L2, and L3 Constructs in CDK?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

L1 (Cfn*): 1:1 CloudFormation mapping. L2: Curated with defaults and helper methods. L3: Patterns — pre-built architectures. Always prefer L2 unless you need properties only available in L1.

Q3

What does cdk bootstrap do?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Creates a dedicated S3 bucket and IAM roles in your AWS account needed for CDK deployments. The bootstrap stack stores assets (Lambda code, Docker images) and provides the execution role for CloudFormation. Run once per account/region.

Q4

How do you write unit tests for CDK infrastructure?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Use cdk.assertions.Template in pytest/jest. Synthesize the stack, then assert: "the template has a resource of type AWS::S3::Bucket with BucketEncryption enabled." This validates infrastructure code without deploying to AWS.

Q5

What is the CDK App → Stack → Construct hierarchy?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

App: root container (one per application). Stack: unit of deployment (maps to CloudFormation stack). Construct: building block (resources or groups). An App contains Stacks; Stacks contain Constructs.

Q6

How can CDK enforce security policies across all stacks?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Use CDK Aspects — they visit every construct in the tree and can add or modify properties. Example: an Aspect that ensures all S3 buckets have encryption enabled and public access blocked. Also cdk-nag for AWS Solutions rules compliance.

Q7

What's the advantage of CDK Pipelines over raw CodePipeline?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

CDK Pipelines automatically defines the pipeline in CDK code, self-mutating — when you update the pipeline definition, the pipeline updates itself. Multi-environment bootstrapping (dev→staging→prod) is built-in with automatic account/region promotion.

Q8

How does CDK handle environment-specific configuration?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Pass context via cdk.json or --context flag. Use this.node.tryGetContext('key') in code. For multi-environment: create separate Stack instances with different env properties (account + region). CDK Pipelines automates this.

Q9

What is cdk diff used for?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Shows the difference between the currently deployed stack and the local CDK code — similar to terraform plan or CloudFormation Change Sets. Critical safety check before cdk deploy to see exactly what will change.

Q10

Which CDK Construct should you use for deploying Kubernetes manifests to EKS?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

cluster.add_manifest() for simple YAML manifests. For Helm charts: use the HelmChart construct or cluster.add_helm_chart(). Both deploy resources after the cluster is provisioned and the kubeconfig is available.

⏱️ 20 min
🏋️ Drill 17: Deploy EKS with CDK

Write a Python CDK stack that: creates a VPC, provisions an EKS cluster with managed node group, deploys the anihpj Deployment and Service. Run cdk synth, review the generated CloudFormation template, then cdk deploy.

Chapter 18: Terraform on AWS — Multi-Cloud IaC

Terraform by HashiCorp is the industry standard for multi-cloud Infrastructure as Code. Use HCL to define resources across AWS, Azure, GCP, and more — with a single workflow. The Terraform Registry hosts thousands of community modules, including production-grade EKS configurations.

🟡 Intermediate ⏱️ ~3 hours 🎯 Domain 2: Config Mgmt 17% 📝 10 Questions

18.1 Terraform vs CloudFormation vs CDK

Terraform by HashiCorp is a multi-cloud IaC tool using HCL (HashiCorp Configuration Language). Unlike CloudFormation (AWS-only), Terraform works across AWS, Azure, GCP, and more:

CriteriaCloudFormationCDKTerraform
LanguageYAML / JSONPython, TS, Java, C#, GoHCL (HashiCorp Language)
Cloud CoverageAWS onlyAWS only (synth to CFN)Multi-cloud (AWS, Azure, GCP, etc.)
State ManagementManaged by AWS (no state files)Managed by AWS (via CFN)You manage state (S3 + DynamoDB)
Drift DetectionBuilt-in (CloudFormation Drift)Via CloudFormation Driftterraform plan shows drift
AbstractionLow (Nested Stacks, Macros)High (L1/L2/L3 Constructs)Medium (Modules, count, for_each)
EcosystemAWS-native, deep integrationAWS-native, Construct HubLargest IaC ecosystem (Terraform Registry)

18.2 Terraform Workflow & State

Terraform's workflow follows a strict pattern. Every change goes through the same cycle:

1
terraform init

Initializes the working directory. Downloads required providers (AWS, Kubernetes, Helm). Sets up the backend for state storage (S3 + DynamoDB for locking). Run once per new workspace. Output: .terraform/ directory with provider binaries.

2
terraform plan

Compares desired state (your .tf files) with actual state (the state file). Shows exactly what will be created (+), modified (~), or destroyed (-). Always review before applying to production. Output can be saved to a file for later apply.

3
terraform apply

Executes the plan. Provisions, updates, or deletes resources. Updates the state file to reflect the new actual state. Production applies should always use a saved plan file (-auto-approve skips confirmation — risky).

⚠️ CloudFormation: No Plan Preview

CloudFormation uses Change Sets (must be explicitly created and reviewed). There's no equivalent to terraform plan that runs automatically before apply. Change Sets are the closest but require an extra step.

✅ Terraform: Plan is the Default

terraform plan runs automatically as part of terraform apply (you see the plan, then confirm). This makes it harder to accidentally apply changes without reviewing them first — a safety advantage over CloudFormation.

🧩 DEEP DIVE: Terraform Modules & Workspaces

Terraform modules are reusable infrastructure packages — like functions for infrastructure. Workspaces let you manage multiple environments from the same configuration:

📦
Modules — Reusable IaC Packages

Encapsulate groups of resources with inputs and outputs. The official terraform-aws-modules/eks/aws module provisions a complete EKS cluster with one module block. Publish to the Terraform Registry for team sharing. Compose modules: VPC module → EKS module → anihpj module.

🌍
Workspaces — Multi-Environment Management

Each workspace has its own state file. terraform workspace new dev creates an isolated environment. Use terraform.workspace variable in your config to switch CIDR ranges, instance sizes, or replica counts per environment. Alternative: separate directory per environment (more explicit, preferred for production).

Terraform State Backend — S3 + DynamoDB Locking
terraform {
  backend "s3" {
    bucket         = "anihpj-terraform-state"
    key            = "prod/eks/terraform.tfstate"
    region         = "us-east-1"
    dynamodb_table = "terraform-state-lock"
    encrypt        = true
  }
}

18.3 Terraform for EKS — anihpj Example

Terraform — EKS Cluster for anihpj
provider "aws" { region = "us-east-1" }

module "eks" {
  source  = "terraform-aws-modules/eks/aws"
  version = "19.21.0"

  cluster_name    = "anihpj-cluster"
  cluster_version = "1.29"

  vpc_id     = module.vpc.vpc_id
  subnet_ids = module.vpc.private_subnets

  eks_managed_node_groups = {
    anihpj = {
      instance_types = ["t3.medium"]
      min_size = 2; desired_size = 3; max_size = 6
    }
  }
}

# Deploy anihpj via Kubernetes provider
provider "kubernetes" {
  host                   = module.eks.cluster_endpoint
  cluster_ca_certificate = base64decode(module.eks.cluster_certificate_authority_data)
  exec { api_version = "client.authentication.k8s.io/v1beta1"
    command = "aws"; args = ["eks", "get-token", "--cluster-name", "anihpj-cluster"] }
}

resource "kubernetes_deployment" "anihpj" {
  metadata { name = "anihpj"; labels = { app = "anihpj" } }
  spec {
    replicas = 3
    selector { match_labels = { app = "anihpj" } }
    template {
      metadata { labels = { app = "anihpj" } }
      spec {
        container {
          name  = "anihpj"
          image = "123456789.dkr.ecr.us-east-1.amazonaws.com/anihpj:v1"
          port  { container_port = 8000 }
        }
      }
    }
  }
}
🔬 DEEP DIVE: Terraform State — The Heart of Terraform

Terraform's state file is its most critical component. Losing it means losing the mapping between your code and real infrastructure:

⚠️ Without Remote State
  • State stored locally on one developer's laptop
  • No team collaboration — only one person can apply
  • Laptop dies → state is gone → resources become unmanaged
  • No state locking → two simultaneous applies corrupt the state
✅ With S3 + DynamoDB
  • State in S3 with versioning — every change is recoverable
  • DynamoDB locks state during operations — no race conditions
  • Team collaboration: plan from anywhere, apply one at a time
  • CI/CD pipeline reads/writes state securely via IAM roles

Chapter 19: AWS Config & Systems Manager — Compliance & Operations

AWS Config tracks every resource change and validates compliance. Systems Manager lets you manage EC2 instances at scale — run commands, patch OS, and access shells — all without SSH. Together they form the operational backbone of a well-governed AWS environment.

🟡 Intermediate ⏱️ ~3 hours 🎯 Domain 2: Config Mgmt 17% & Domain 4: Monitoring 15% 📝 10 Questions

19.1 AWS Config — Resource Compliance

AWS Config continuously monitors and records your AWS resource configurations. It tracks every change — who made it, when, and what changed:

📋
Resource Inventory

Complete inventory of all AWS resources with configuration history. "What did my security group look like last Tuesday?" Config has the answer — full timeline of every configuration change.

✅
Config Rules

Continuously evaluate resources against compliance rules. AWS-managed rules (S3_BUCKET_PUBLIC_READ_PROHIBITED) or custom Lambda-based rules. Auto-remediate: trigger SSM Automation on non-compliance.

📦
Conformance Packs

Bundles of Config rules + remediations deployed as a single unit. Compliance as code — version the pack in Git. Example: "Operational Best Practices for EKS" conformance pack.

🔄 How AWS Config Works — From Resource Change to Compliance
1. Record Configuration

Every time a resource changes (create, update, delete), Config captures a configuration snapshot — the full JSON representation of the resource at that moment. This builds a complete timeline.

2. Evaluate Rules

Config runs all applicable rules against the new configuration. Managed rules like S3_BUCKET_PUBLIC_READ_PROHIBITED check compliance. Custom Lambda rules can check anything accessible via AWS API.

3. Report Compliance

Results flow to the compliance dashboard: COMPLIANT (green) or NON_COMPLIANT (red). Aggregated across all accounts and regions if using an aggregator.

4. Auto-Remediate (Optional)

If a rule has a remediation action, Config triggers the associated SSM Automation runbook. Example: S3 bucket becomes public → auto-trigger AWS-ConfigureS3BucketPublicAccessBlock to block it.

19.2 Systems Manager — Operational Hub

AWS Systems Manager (SSM) is the central operational hub for managing EC2 instances, on-prem servers, and hybrid environments — all without SSH:

🏃
Run Command

Execute scripts or commands on managed instances without SSH. IAM-controlled, CloudTrail-audited. Run a script on 1,000 instances simultaneously with rate control and error thresholds.

🔧
Patch Manager

Automate OS patching on a schedule. Define patch baselines, maintenance windows, and patch compliance reporting. Critical for security compliance — prove all instances are patched.

🔒
Session Manager

Browser-based shell access to EC2 instances — no open inbound ports, no SSH keys, no bastion hosts. All sessions logged to CloudTrail + S3 + CloudWatch Logs for full audit trail.

⚖️ Parameter Store vs Secrets Manager — When to Use Which
FeatureParameter StoreSecrets Manager
CostFree (Standard), $0.05/advanced$0.40/secret/month + API charges
Automatic Rotation❌ Manual only✅ Built-in Lambda rotation (RDS, Redshift, DocumentDB)
