Cross-Account Access❌ No native support✅ Resource-based policies
Max Size4 KB (Standard), 8 KB (Advanced)64 KB
JSON SupportLimited (String only, parse manually)✅ Native JSON — store multiple key-values in one secret
Use CaseConfig values, AMI IDs, feature flagsDatabase passwords, API keys, OAuth tokens

19.3 Automation Documents & Runbooks

SSM Automation lets you define runbooks — pre-approved operational procedures that run without human intervention:

SSM Automation Document — Restart Unhealthy anihpj Instance
schemaVersion: "0.3"
description: "Restart unhealthy anihpj EC2 instance"
assumeRole: "{{AutomationAssumeRole}}"
parameters:
  InstanceId:
    type: String
    description: "Instance to restart"
mainSteps:
  - name: StopInstance
    action: aws:changeInstanceState
    inputs:
      InstanceIds: ["{{InstanceId}}"]
      DesiredState: stopped
  - name: WaitForStop
    action: aws:waitForAwsResourceProperty
    inputs:
      Service: ec2; Api: DescribeInstances
      InstanceIds: ["{{InstanceId}}"]
      PropertySelector: "$.Reservations[0].Instances[0].State.Name"
      DesiredValues: ["stopped"]
  - name: StartInstance
    action: aws:changeInstanceState
    inputs:
      InstanceIds: ["{{InstanceId}}"]
      DesiredState: running
🔒 DEEP DIVE: Session Manager vs SSH — The Security Revolution
🔓 Traditional SSH
  • Port 22 open to the internet or bastion host
  • SSH key pairs to manage, rotate, and secure
  • Bastion host = additional attack surface + cost
  • No built-in audit trail — who did what, when?
  • Security groups must allow inbound 0.0.0.0/0 or specific IPs
🔒 SSM Session Manager
  • No open inbound ports — outbound HTTPS only
  • No SSH keys — IAM controls who can start sessions
  • No bastion host — Session Manager proxies through SSM service
  • Full audit trail: CloudTrail (who), S3 (what they typed), CloudWatch Logs (output)
  • Works on EC2, on-prem, and hybrid environments

Chapter 19B: AWS Elastic Beanstalk — Managed Application Platform

Elastic Beanstalk is AWS's Platform-as-a-Service — upload your code, and Beanstalk handles capacity provisioning, load balancing, auto-scaling, and health monitoring. It's the fastest path from code to production for traditional web applications, with no container or Kubernetes knowledge required.

🟡 Intermediate ⏱️ ~2 hours 🎯 Domain 2: Config Mgmt 17% 📝 10 Questions

19B.1 Elastic Beanstalk Overview

AWS Elastic Beanstalk is a PaaS that handles the heavy lifting: capacity provisioning, load balancing, auto-scaling, and health monitoring. You upload your code — Beanstalk handles the rest:

🚀
PaaS Simplicity

Upload code → Beanstalk provisions: EC2 instances, ASG, ALB, security groups, CloudWatch monitoring. Focus on your application, not the infrastructure.

🐍
Platform Support

Python, Node.js, Java, .NET, Go, PHP, Ruby, Docker (single/multi-container). Pre-configured platform with the runtime, proxy (nginx/Apache), and health agent pre-installed.

⚖️
EB vs ECS vs EKS vs Lambda

EB: PaaS, simplest. ECS: container orchestration (AWS-native). EKS: full Kubernetes. Lambda: serverless. Choose EB when you want PaaS simplicity with managed infrastructure.

🤔 Beanstalk or EKS? — The Decision Framework
✅ Choose Beanstalk When:
  • Traditional web app (Django, Rails, Express) — upload code and go
  • No container expertise — Beanstalk abstracts all infrastructure
  • Fast time-to-market — eb create puts you in production in minutes
  • Managed platform updates — Beanstalk patches the OS and runtime for you
❌ Choose EKS When:
  • Microservices with independent scaling, service mesh, custom networking
  • GitOps (ArgoCD/Flux) with declarative cluster state management
  • Multi-cloud portability — Kubernetes runs anywhere
  • Full ecosystem — Helm, operators, CRDs, extensive community tooling

19B.2 Deploying anihpj on Beanstalk

Elastic Beanstalk — anihpj Deployment
# Install EB CLI
pip install awsebcli

# Initialize Beanstalk application
eb init -p python-3.12 anihpj --region us-east-1

# Create environment (with load balancer, auto-scaling)
eb create anihpj-prod --instance-type t3.medium --min-instances 2 --max-instances 6

# Deploy application
eb deploy

# View status and logs
eb status && eb logs

# .ebextensions/django.config — custom configuration
# files placed in .ebextensions/ are applied during deployment
container_commands:
  01_migrate:
    command: "python manage.py migrate --noinput"
    leader_only: true
  02_collectstatic:
    command: "python manage.py collectstatic --noinput"

19B.3 Deployment Modes

ModeDowntimeSpeedRollback
All at OnceBriefFastestRedeploy previous
RollingZeroMediumReverse rollout
Rolling with Add'l BatchZeroSlowerReverse rollout
ImmutableZeroSlowestTerminate new, keep old
Traffic Splitting (Canary)ZeroSlowAuto via CloudWatch
Blue/Green (Clone + Swap)ZeroMediumSwap URLs back

19B.4 High Availability

Beanstalk automatically provides HA when you configure a load-balanced environment: Multi-AZ deployment across at least 2 AZs, auto-scaling based on CPU/memory/request count, health-based instance replacement, and managed platform updates with immutable deployments to prevent patching failures.

🔧 DEEP DIVE: Beanstalk Extensions & Troubleshooting
📦
.ebextensions Configuration

Place .config files in .ebextensions/ directory. Define: packages to install, files to create, commands to run (leader_only for DB migrations), services to start, and CloudFormation resources to add. Applied in alphabetical order during deployment.

🔍
Troubleshooting Commands

eb logs — tail environment logs. eb health — instance + environment health. eb status — current environment state. Health Dashboard: Green (OK), Yellow (Warning), Red (Degraded). Check CloudWatch for detailed metrics on 5xx errors, latency, and request rate.

Chapter 19C: AWS SAM & Serverless Deployments

The Serverless Application Model extends CloudFormation with serverless-optimized syntax. Define Lambda functions, API Gateway APIs, and DynamoDB tables in a fraction of the CloudFormation YAML. Integrated with CodeDeploy for canary traffic shifting and automatic rollback on your serverless applications.

🟡 Intermediate ⏱️ ~2 hours 🎯 Domain 2: Config Mgmt 17% & Domain 1: SDLC 22% 📝 10 Questions

19C.1 SAM Overview

AWS SAM (Serverless Application Model) is a CloudFormation extension optimized for serverless. It provides shorthand syntax for Lambda, API Gateway, and DynamoDB — sam build && sam deploy handles everything.

SAM reduces a 200-line CloudFormation template to ~25 lines. AWS::Serverless::Function expands to: Lambda function + IAM execution role + CloudWatch Log Group + API Gateway integration + event source mapping — all from one resource declaration. This is why SAM is the standard for serverless IaC on AWS.

SAM Template — anihpj Job API
AWSTemplateFormatVersion: "2010-09-09"
Transform: AWS::Serverless-2016-10-31

Resources:
  JobPostFunction:
    Type: AWS::Serverless::Function
    Properties:
      CodeUri: jobpost/
      Handler: app.lambda_handler
      Runtime: python3.12
      Events:
        GetJobs:
          Type: Api
          Properties:
            Path: /jobs
            Method: get

  JobPostTable:
    Type: AWS::Serverless::SimpleTable
    Properties:
      PrimaryKey: {Name: id, Type: String}

19C.2 SAM with CodeDeploy — Gradual Deployments

SAM natively supports CodeDeploy for safe Lambda deployments with traffic shifting and automatic rollback via CloudWatch alarms:

📝sam buildBuild + package
→
🚀sam deployCreate new Lambda version
→
🐤10% TrafficCanary shift via alias
→
📊CloudWatchMonitor errors/latency
→
✅100% TrafficPromote or auto-rollback

Configure DeploymentPreference in the SAM template with canary or linear strategies. SAM auto-creates Lambda versions, configures the alias with weighted routing, and sets up CloudWatch alarms for automatic rollback — all from a single configuration block.

19C.3 Lambda DevOps Deep Dive

🏷️
Versions & Aliases

Versions: immutable snapshots of function code + config. Aliases: mutable pointers to versions (PROD → v3). CodeDeploy shifts traffic between versions via alias weight changes.

🌐
Lambda in VPC

Attach Lambda to private subnets to access RDS, ElastiCache, or internal APIs. Lambda creates ENIs in your VPC. Use VPC Endpoints for DynamoDB/S3 to avoid NAT Gateway costs.

📊
Reserved + Provisioned Concurrency

Reserved: guarantees capacity (throttles other functions). Provisioned: keeps execution environments warm — eliminates cold starts for latency-sensitive APIs.

19C.4 API Gateway Deep Dive

TypeFeaturesBest For
🔌 REST APIAPI keys, usage plans, request/response transformation, response caching. Full API management features.Public APIs with monetization, complex validation, or third-party developer portals
⚡ HTTP API70% cheaper, lower latency. No API keys or usage plans — use IAM auth or JWT authorizers instead.Internal APIs, Lambda proxies, simple routing where cost and speed matter more than features
🐤 Canary DeploymentsDeploy to a canary stage, route X% of traffic via stage variables, monitor CloudWatch metrics. Promote or rollback based on results.Safe API changes with minimal blast radius — test with real traffic before full rollout
⚡ DEEP DIVE: Step Functions — Serverless Orchestration

AWS Step Functions lets you build visual workflows that coordinate multiple AWS services. For DevOps, it's the standard for complex CI/CD orchestration beyond linear pipelines:

🔀 State Machines

Define workflows as JSON (Amazon States Language). States: Task, Choice, Parallel, Wait, Succeed/Fail. Visual execution in the console — see exactly which step succeeded or failed. Standard workflows guarantee exactly-once execution for up to 1 year.

⚡ Express Workflows

At-least-once execution, up to 5 minutes, high throughput. Ideal for event processing, streaming data, and high-volume workloads. Standard = audit trail + long-running. Express = speed + throughput.

CodePipeline pauses at approval stage

The pipeline reaches a manual approval gate — it needs a human to approve before deploying to production.

Step Function triggered by CloudWatch Event

A Step Functions state machine starts: it sends a Slack notification with deployment details and Approve/Reject buttons.

Wait for approval (up to 7 days)

The state machine waits with a Task token. The engineer clicks Approve in Slack, which calls a Lambda that sends the SendTaskSuccess API.

Signal CodePipeline to continue

Step Function receives the approval, signals CodePipeline via putApprovalResult, and the pipeline proceeds to production deployment.

This pattern — CodePipeline + Step Functions + SNS/Slack — is the standard for auditable, multi-step CI/CD approval workflows on AWS. Step Functions has 200+ direct service integrations, allowing it to orchestrate Lambda, ECS, EKS, CodeBuild, SNS, DynamoDB, and more without writing glue code.

📝 Chapter 17 — AWS CDK (10 Questions)

Q1

What is the relationship between CDK and CloudFormation?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

CDK synthesizes code into CloudFormation templates. cdk synth generates a JSON/YAML template. cdk deploy submits it to CloudFormation. CDK is a higher-level abstraction — it writes CloudFormation for you.

Q2

What are L1, L2, and L3 Constructs in CDK?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

L1 (Cfn*): 1:1 CloudFormation mapping. L2: Curated with defaults and helper methods. L3: Patterns — pre-built architectures. Always prefer L2 unless you need properties only available in L1.

Q3

What does cdk bootstrap do?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Creates a dedicated S3 bucket and IAM roles in your AWS account needed for CDK deployments. The bootstrap stack stores assets (Lambda code, Docker images) and provides the execution role for CloudFormation. Run once per account/region.

Q4

How do you write unit tests for CDK infrastructure?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Use cdk.assertions.Template in pytest/jest. Synthesize the stack, then assert: "the template has a resource of type AWS::S3::Bucket with BucketEncryption enabled." This validates infrastructure code without deploying to AWS.

Q5

What is the CDK App → Stack → Construct hierarchy?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

App: root container (one per application). Stack: unit of deployment (maps to CloudFormation stack). Construct: building block (resources or groups). An App contains Stacks; Stacks contain Constructs.

Q6

How can CDK enforce security policies across all stacks?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Use CDK Aspects — they visit every construct in the tree and can add or modify properties. Example: an Aspect that ensures all S3 buckets have encryption enabled and public access blocked. Also cdk-nag for AWS Solutions rules compliance.

Q7

What's the advantage of CDK Pipelines over raw CodePipeline?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

CDK Pipelines automatically defines the pipeline in CDK code, self-mutating — when you update the pipeline definition, the pipeline updates itself. Multi-environment bootstrapping (dev→staging→prod) is built-in with automatic account/region promotion.

Q8

How does CDK handle environment-specific configuration?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Pass context via cdk.json or --context flag. Use this.node.tryGetContext('key') in code. For multi-environment: create separate Stack instances with different env properties (account + region). CDK Pipelines automates this.

Q9

What is cdk diff used for?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Shows the difference between the currently deployed stack and the local CDK code — similar to terraform plan or CloudFormation Change Sets. Critical safety check before cdk deploy to see exactly what will change.

Q10

Which CDK Construct should you use for deploying Kubernetes manifests to EKS?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

cluster.add_manifest() for simple YAML manifests. For Helm charts: use the HelmChart construct or cluster.add_helm_chart(). Both deploy resources after the cluster is provisioned and the kubeconfig is available.

⏱️ 20 min
🏋️ Drill 17: Deploy EKS with CDK

Write a Python CDK stack that: creates a VPC, provisions an EKS cluster with managed node group, deploys the anihpj Deployment and Service. Run cdk synth, review the generated CloudFormation template, then cdk deploy.

📝 Chapter 18 — Terraform on AWS (10 Questions)

Q1

What is the purpose of Terraform state?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

State maps Terraform resources to real-world resources. It tracks metadata (resource dependencies, outputs) and enables terraform plan to compare desired vs actual. Store state remotely (S3 + DynamoDB locking) for team collaboration.

Q2

Why use DynamoDB with S3 for Terraform state?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

DynamoDB provides state locking — prevents two people from running terraform apply simultaneously, which would corrupt the state. S3 stores the state file. The DynamoDB table holds a lock during operations.

Q3

What does terraform plan do?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Compares the desired state (your .tf files) with the actual state (state file) and outputs a diff: resources to create (+), modify (~), or destroy (-). Always review the plan before applying to production.

Q4

What is a Terraform Provider?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

A plugin that translates Terraform's declarative language into API calls for a specific platform (AWS, Kubernetes, Helm, GitHub). The AWS provider maps Terraform resources to CloudFormation API calls behind the scenes.

Q5

How does Terraform handle resource dependencies?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Terraform builds a dependency graph automatically. When resource A references resource B (e.g., subnet_ids = module.vpc.private_subnets), Terraform knows to create B first. Use depends_on for explicit dependencies Terraform can't infer.

Q6

What is terraform import used for?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Imports an existing resource (created manually or by another tool) into Terraform state so Terraform can manage it. You write the resource block in your config, then terraform import aws_instance.example i-abc123 — Terraform now manages the instance.

Q7

What are Terraform modules?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Reusable, composable packages of Terraform resources. The EKS module (terraform-aws-modules/eks/aws) provisions a complete EKS cluster with one module call. Modules encapsulate best practices and reduce duplication.

Q8

What happens if the Terraform state file is lost?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Infrastructure becomes unmanaged. Terraform can't track or update resources without state. Recovery: restore from S3 versioning, or use terraform import to re-import every resource individually. This is why you ALWAYS enable S3 versioning on the state bucket.

Q9

How does Terraform differ from CloudFormation for multi-cloud?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Terraform is cloud-agnostic — one language (HCL), one workflow, any cloud. CloudFormation is AWS-only. If you need to manage resources across AWS + Azure + GCP, Terraform is the standard. CloudFormation is better for AWS-native, deeply integrated workflows.

Q10

What command validates Terraform syntax without connecting to any services?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

terraform validate — checks that the configuration is syntactically valid and internally consistent. Does NOT check against remote services (no API calls). Run it in CI/CD before terraform plan to catch syntax errors early.

⏱️ 20 min
🏋️ Drill 18: Terraform EKS from Scratch

Write Terraform config to: create VPC module, provision EKS cluster with managed node group, configure S3+DynamoDB state backend, deploy anihpj using the Kubernetes provider. Run terraform init && terraform plan && terraform apply.

📝 Chapter 19 — AWS Config & Systems Manager (10 Questions)

Q1

What does AWS Config continuously monitor?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Resource configurations and their changes over time. Config records: what the resource looked like at any point in time, who changed it, when, and what changed. It provides a full configuration timeline for compliance auditing.

Q2

How does SSM Session Manager improve security over SSH?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Session Manager requires: no open inbound ports (outbound HTTPS only), no SSH keys, no bastion hosts. All sessions authenticated via IAM and fully logged to CloudTrail + S3 + CloudWatch. SSH requires port 22 open, key management, and bastion infrastructure.

Q3

What is the difference between SSM Parameter Store and Secrets Manager?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Parameter Store: free for standard params, no automatic rotation. Secrets Manager: paid, automatic rotation via Lambda, cross-account access, JSON secret support. Use Secrets Manager for database credentials, API keys requiring rotation.

Q4

How can AWS Config automatically fix non-compliant resources?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Use auto-remediation with SSM Automation documents. When a Config rule detects non-compliance, it triggers an SSM Automation runbook that fixes the resource (e.g., "S3 bucket is public → run automation to block public access").

Q5

What is SSM Run Command?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Executes scripts or commands on managed EC2 instances without SSH. IAM-controlled, CloudTrail-audited. Execute on 1,000+ instances simultaneously with rate control and error thresholds. No open inbound ports needed.

Q6

What is an SSM Automation Runbook?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

A pre-approved, documented operational procedure that runs without human intervention. Example: "Restart unhealthy instance" — stop → wait → start → verify. Runbooks are YAML/JSON documents executed by SSM Automation, triggered manually or by CloudWatch Alarms/EventBridge.

Q7

How does SSM Patch Manager help with compliance?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Automates OS patching on a schedule with defined maintenance windows. Patch baselines define which patches to apply. Compliance reports show which instances are patched vs missing patches. Critical for security audits and compliance frameworks.

Q8

What is a Config Conformance Pack?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

A bundle of Config rules + remediation actions deployed as a single YAML template. "Compliance as code" — version the pack in Git. AWS provides pre-built packs like "Operational Best Practices for EKS" for common compliance frameworks.

Q9

How does SSM State Manager enforce desired configuration?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

State Manager defines desired states (associations) for managed instances — "run this script every 30 minutes," "ensure this service is running." It continuously applies and verifies the configuration, correcting drift automatically.

Q10

What IAM permissions does SSM Session Manager require on EC2 instances?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

The instance profile needs ssm:UpdateInstanceInformation, ssmmessages:*, ec2messages:*, and s3:GetObject (for Session Manager S3 logging). The IAM user initiating the session needs ssm:StartSession on the target instance.

⏱️ 15 min
🏋️ Drill 19: SSM + Config for anihpj

Configure AWS Config rules to monitor anihpj S3 buckets for public access. Create an SSM Automation runbook that restarts unhealthy anihpj EC2 instances. Set up Session Manager and access an instance without SSH.

📝 Chapter 19B — Elastic Beanstalk (10 Questions)

Q1

What is AWS Elastic Beanstalk's primary value proposition?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

PaaS simplicity: upload your code, Beanstalk handles everything — capacity provisioning, load balancing, auto-scaling, health monitoring. You focus on code, not infrastructure. Under the hood: EC2, ASG, ALB, CloudWatch — all managed for you.

Q2

What is the difference between Beanstalk Web Server and Worker tiers?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Web Server: handles HTTP requests, ALB in front, auto-scaling of EC2 instances. Worker: processes messages from SQS queue, no ALB, scales based on queue depth. Use Worker tier for background job processing (e.g., resume parsing).

Q3

How does Beanstalk Immutable deployment work?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Creates a completely new ASG with the new version, launches instances, runs health checks. If healthy, swaps the new ASG into the ALB. If unhealthy, the new ASG is terminated — old ASG untouched. Zero downtime, instant rollback.

Q4

What is .ebextensions used for?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

A directory in your application source containing .config files that customize Beanstalk environments. Define: packages to install, files to create, commands to run, services to start, and CloudFormation resources to add. Applied during deployment.

Q5

How does Beanstalk handle Blue/Green deployments?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Clone environment, then swap URLs. Clone the current environment to create an identical Green environment. Deploy the new version to Green. Test Green. Use "Swap Environment URLs" to instantly switch traffic. Rollback: swap back.

Q6

What platforms does Beanstalk support?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Python, Node.js, Java (Tomcat/SE), .NET, Go, PHP, Ruby, Docker (single/multi-container). Each platform includes a pre-configured runtime, reverse proxy (nginx/Apache), and health agent. Custom platforms possible via Packer.

Q7

How does Beanstalk managed platform updates work?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Beanstalk automatically applies minor platform updates during maintenance windows. Major version updates require explicit action. Updates use the configured deployment strategy (rolling, immutable) to minimize downtime.

Q8

When would you choose Beanstalk over ECS/EKS?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Beanstalk: no container knowledge needed, simplest deployment model, built for traditional web apps. ECS: container-native, more control. EKS: full Kubernetes ecosystem. Choose Beanstalk for maximum simplicity with managed infrastructure.

Q9

What is the Beanstalk Health Dashboard?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

A centralized view of environment health across all Beanstalk applications. Colors: Green (OK), Yellow (Warning), Red (Degraded), Grey (Unknown). Monitors 10+ metrics including request rate, latency, 5xx errors, and instance health.

Q10

How do you deploy a Django app like anihpj to Beanstalk?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Install EB CLI. Run eb init -p python-3.12 anihpj. Create .ebextensions/django.config with migrate and collectstatic commands. Run eb create anihpj-prod. Deploy updates with eb deploy. Configure RDS via environment properties.

📝 Chapter 19C — SAM & Serverless (10 Questions)

Q1

What is the relationship between SAM and CloudFormation?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

SAM is a CloudFormation extension with a transform (AWS::Serverless-2016-10-31). SAM shorthand (e.g., AWS::Serverless::Function) expands to full CloudFormation resources during sam build. SAM = CloudFormation + serverless-optimized syntax.

Q2

How does SAM handle Lambda deployments with CodeDeploy?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Add DeploymentPreference to the function resource with canary/linear strategy and CloudWatch alarms. SAM auto-creates Lambda versions, aliases, and CodeDeploy resources. Traffic shifts gradually; auto-rollback on alarm trigger.

Q3

What does sam local invoke do?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Runs a Lambda function locally in a Docker container that emulates the Lambda runtime. Pass test events via -e event.json. Enables local debugging and fast iteration without deploying to AWS. Also supports sam local start-api for API Gateway emulation.

Q4

What is the difference between Lambda versions and aliases?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Versions: immutable snapshots of function code + config ($LATEST → 1, 2, 3). Aliases: mutable pointers to versions (PROD → v3). CodeDeploy shifts traffic by updating alias weights between two versions.

Q5

Why would you place a Lambda function in a VPC?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

To access private VPC resources: RDS databases, ElastiCache clusters, internal APIs. The Lambda creates ENIs in your private subnets. Use VPC Endpoints for S3/DynamoDB to avoid NAT Gateway costs for AWS service access.

Q6

What is the difference between REST API and HTTP API in API Gateway?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

REST API: full-featured — API keys, usage plans, request validation, caching. HTTP API: lightweight, 70% cheaper, lower latency. No API keys/usage plans. Best for internal APIs and Lambda proxies where simplicity matters.

Q7

How does API Gateway handle canary deployments?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Create a canary stage deployment with a traffic percentage (e.g., 10%). Monitor the canary via CloudWatch metrics. If healthy, promote the canary to the stage (100%). If unhealthy, delete the canary — traffic returns to the stable stage deployment.

Q8

What SAM resource type creates a DynamoDB table?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

AWS::Serverless::SimpleTable — creates a DynamoDB table with a single primary key, pay-per-request billing, and encryption enabled by default. For advanced use (GSI, LSI, provisioned capacity), use the full AWS::DynamoDB::Table resource.

Q9

What is the SAM template transform?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

Transform: AWS::Serverless-2016-10-31 — tells CloudFormation to process SAM-specific resource types and expand them into full CloudFormation resources. Without this transform, SAM shorthand is not recognized.

Q10

How do you set up API Gateway caching in SAM?

Show Answer
✅ ANSWER

In the AWS::Serverless::Api resource, set CacheClusterEnabled: true and CacheClusterSize: "0.5" (GB). Define caching per stage. API Gateway caches responses for configured TTL — reducing Lambda invocations and latency for repeated requests.

⏱️ 20 min
🏋️ Drill 19B: Deploy anihpj on Elastic Beanstalk

Package anihpj for Beanstalk: create .ebextensions/django.config, run eb init + eb create, configure environment properties for RDS, deploy and verify the application is running. Test an Immutable deployment with a config change.

⏱️ 20 min
🏋️ Drill 19C: SAM Deploy of anihpj API

Write a SAM template for the anihpj jobpost API: Lambda function + API Gateway HTTP API + DynamoDB table. Add CodeDeploy canary deployment preference. Test locally with sam local start-api, then sam deploy --guided.
